› Message from general-purpose
  **Standards review, PR #290 (#246)**

  **Hard violations**

  1. **The request door has no rate limit.** `Inquiry/Handler.php` checks only
  the nonce and `PublicGuard`. A guest nonce is the same for every visitor (uid
  0), so a bot can fetch it, wait 3 s and post in a loop. The contact door keeps
  its per-connection limit (`Contact/Handler::count()/LIMIT/WINDOW`). The
  proforma door has no equivalent. Each post sends a receipt to an address the
  attacker chooses, plus a team mail. That makes the site a mail relay and can
  use up the shared Hostinger quota of 100 mails per 24 h (AGENTS.md §4 邮件).
  The spec's own text confirms that only the contact door is limited
  (11-commerce.md, 联系页 "原每连接限流").
  2. **Dead code left behind** (AGENTS.md §5 本轮发现本轮修):
     - `Checkout::feedback` (inline JS aimed at `form.checkout`),
  `review_language`, `language_field` and `reply_promise` are still hooked.
  Native checkout now never renders and is rejected in `validate_public`.
     - `LoginGate::html()` and the private gates only it reaches
  (`empty_draft_gate`, `proforma_gate`, `guest_draft_gate`) lost their only
  caller in `Shortcodes::checkout`.
     - `ProductView` still holds the `login_url`/`Approval` paths that served
  the removed CTA branches. Check whether `login_url` is still needed at line
  293.
     - The `gateways()` docblock still says "only to accounts that may send".
  3. **Unsourced numbers and a raw palette in `content/windpress/auth.css` (#246
  block)** (AGENTS.md §4 "每个数字与阈值必须有来源注释"):
     - It uses raw hex values (`#087f75`, `#647b78`, `#d7e0e5`, `#a33434`,
  `#066c63`) and px values (32/40/72/142/48…) with no source.
     - The same file states its aim is to "Replace the contact shortcode's
  literal palette with the site's tokens", and the success block just above uses
  `var(--proto-*)`.
  4. **i18n:** the honeypot's `aria-label="Website"` in `inquiry/form.html` is
  untranslated (§3 英德永远同步).

  **Judgement calls / smells**

  - **Duplicated Code.** The language-twin lookup `pll_get_post(...,
  str_starts_with($locale,'de') ? 'de' : 'en')` is repeated in
  `Sender::line_language`, `Checkout::item_permalink`, `Checkout::request_url`,
  `AccountLink::url` and `Form::hidden`.
  - **Flag argument / Divergent Change.** In `Sender::submit(..., bool
  $with_draft)` the flag switches between two separate creation paths:
  `create_order($data)` versus a hand-built `new WC_Order()`. The `$data`
  address/VAT clump is built and then thrown away on the contact path.
  - **Mysterious Name.** `$source` in `OrderAdmin::source()` holds a bool
  meaning "is contact".
  - **Honeypot on one door only.** `PublicGuard::allows()` checks `website`, but
  `PublicGuard::fields()` does not emit it. The trap therefore exists only on
  the request door, and contact relies on its own `TRAP_FIELD`. The guard's
  contract is split.
  - **Guest PII in the session.** After a failed submit, name, email, company
  and message stay in `WC()->session` until the session expires. Nothing clears
  them except a later success. Guest orders follow Woo's retention settings, and
  the spec never states that policy.
  - **Formatting noise.** Many files gain extra blank lines inside classes and
  reflowed `/** */` email comments. This is low priority.

  **Checked and clean**

  - No inline styles. Layout lives in LC fragments, CSS in the WindPress volume,
  and the fragments are registered as a provider.
  - Output is escaped, and customer text is substituted after `do_shortcode`.
  - `is_email` and sanitisation sit in `Submission`.
  - Browser input cannot set products or a customer ID.
  - `AccountLink` requires the `hash_equals` order key, a guest order and a
  matching email.
  - The Store API checkout is blocked.
  - The native-first proof is in the spec.
  - Tests use example.test or allowed addresses only.
  - No legacy vocabulary added.
  - `MIN_SECONDS = 3` is sourced (#246).

› Message from general-purpose
  **Spec review of PR #290 (#246): what's missing, what's wrong, and the 57 px**

  **(a) Missing or partial**
  1. **Restricted accounts no longer see their reason on the site.** Spec:
  "限制用于告诉客户信息不够…原因给客户看" (AGENTS 2026-10-17). `Shortcodes` no
  longer renders `LoginGate::html()`, and nothing else calls it. So the
  restricted card with `reason_notice()` is now dead code, and its copy is
  false: "the Proforma request are paused". `restricted-en-1440.jpg` shows only
  the form and no reason. The reason now reaches the customer only through the
  email. Also stale: the `PendingGate` docblock ("reads its own gate on the
  Proforma request") and the 11-commerce #138 line.
  2. **"Change" opens three reply fields, not the Woo billing fields.** DECISION
  says "'修改'展开 Woo 账单字段". In `form.html` it opens name, email and
  company only. Address and VAT are prefilled in `Sender`, which is allowed
  ("从账号预填"), but the expand behaviour does not match the decision.
  3. **"One record" is left half-switched.** Contact messages now go through
  `Sender` and trigger `InquiryReceived`/`NewInquiry`. But
  `ReceiptEmail`/`TeamMessageEmail` are still registered in Woo's email list and
  never fire. 11-commerce §联系 still describes them. `NotificationRoutingTest`
  still asserts contact routing through the dead `TeamMessageEmail`, so the
  real contact team mail has no routing row there. Spec: "本轮发现的问题本轮修".

  **(b) Scope creep:** none found. Each item traces to a ruling or a necessary
  consequence:
  - Editable quantities: "右边 Lösungsentwurf（可编辑）".
  - Optional registration with the same email prefilled: "感谢页 +
  已收到邮件…用这个邮箱创建账户（一步，选填）". The key-checked claim is a
  necessary safety consequence.
  - The contact form gets only two hidden `PublicGuard` inputs
  ("防机器人与注册同一套").
  - Blocking the Store API checkout is needed for the single entry point.
  - The €0 fix and the desktop thank-you card are bug fixes listed in the change
  ledger.

  **(c) Implemented, but check these**
  - **Layout:** it is the ruled variant B (left form, right draft; signed-in
  details collapse to one line; "No account needed" and a login link). The
  ledger lists it as a ruled change.
  - **#250 can use `Sender` without an HTML form post.** `submit(Submission,
  bool)` is a plain PHP call. Caveats for #250:
    - It must build the `Submission` with `from_post(array)`, including consent.
    - It must call `wc_load_cart()` in REST.
    - It needs its own guard, because `PublicGuard` is shaped for HTML forms.
  Spec allows this: "须保留该调用方的 nonce、bot guard".
  - **#221 routing:** the Outbox test for `notify_to`/Bcc exists in
  `OpenInquiryTest`.

  **The 57 px (EN1440): a measurement artefact, not a contact-form change. The
  gate is still not passed.** Evidence:
  - `contact-form-geometry.json`: the live and local form rectangles are
  identical in all four captures.
  - The PR adds no contact markup or CSS beyond the two hidden inputs.
  - DE1440, EN390 and DE390 form regions are 0.
  - I recomputed the 57 from `live/local-contact-en-1440.png`. All are
  anti-aliasing edge pixels with RGB deltas of 1–11:
    - a 15 px column at x=682, the fractional card edge (x=681.59);
    - corner pixels of the inputs, the checkbox and the button (y 923–1109),
  whose interiors are identical;
    - the card's bottom-right corner.

  Under §5 ("未声明的差异像素即 FAIL"; masks only for real dynamic regions) it
  cannot be waved through. The fix is a fresh capture of both sides in one
  session after the rebase; the current pair predates the rebase.

  The larger blocker is the full contact page: 129,712 differing pixels below
  the toolbar on EN1440, in the hero and footer photo bands. That is outside the
  form and likely image delivery, not this PR. The contact page still fails the
  pixel gate and needs attribution against a main-local baseline.

› Message from general-purpose
  **Spec review, PR #291 (#288)**

  **(a) Missing or partial**
  1. "1. **先查清当前信封发件人。** 读一封站点实发邮件的完整头部…" and
  "本线决定（先测后改）". No "before" headers were captured. The PR defers both
  production criteria ("头部证据：改前和改后各一份",
  "改后…都进**收件箱**，截图") to Claude. Expected, but it means the "before"
  capture has to happen on production **before deploying**. If it doesn't, the
  before/after proof can't be recovered. Today WP calls `setFrom(...,false)`, so
  `Sender` is empty and Hostinger's MTA chooses the envelope. It may already be
  a Hostinger host domain that passes SPF. In that case this change adds
  nothing for SPF, and the junk placement comes from the From domain having no
  DKIM. Step 1 exists to rule that out.
  2. Folded item 1: "本票新增的信封发件人也从同一处取". The envelope is not
  taken from `notify_sender()`. It is a separate option, and only its *default
  constant* aliases `DEFAULT_NOTIFY_FROM`. The PR says this is deliberate
  ("互不覆盖"), and it is the safer design, but it departs from the ticket text.
  The ticket comment should record that decision.
  3. Folded item 4: "用 `wp i18n make-pot` 重新生成，不手写条目". The README
  shows `make-pot --merge=/tmp/c288-existing.pot`, which carries old entries
  forward, including any that were hand-written. Partial.

  **(b) Scope creep**
  None of substance. The ticket only scoped "客户类邮件", but a site-wide Sender
  covers the same change. The ticket gave `bounce@sunriverinverter.com` as its
  example; the default is `sales@sunriverinverter.com` instead, so customer
  bounces land in the internal inbox. Acceptable, but worth a line in spec 11.

  **(c) Looks implemented, possibly wrong**
  - `Sender` via `phpmailer_init` → `-f`: correct. Folded items 2 and 3 are
  correct and tested.
  - **No conflict with #286.** `notify_sender()` wraps `notify_from()`, the From
  values are unchanged, and `notify_from()` stays the single source.
  - DMARC judgement. Expected result: `spf=pass
  smtp.mailfrom=sunriverinverter.com`, but only if the web host's relay IP is
  inside `_spf.mail.hostinger.com`. Production has to confirm that. Also
  `dkim=none` and `dmarc=none` (no record), so nothing is aligned. With no DMARC
  policy, the unaligned pass does no harm and meets Gmail's minimum for
  low-volume senders ("SPF or DKIM pass on any domain"). It is not an aligned
  authentication, though, so receivers like QQ may still filter on reputation
  and heuristics. Gmail will likely show "via sunriverinverter.com" to
  customers, which is a soft exposure next to the 2026-10-17 rule that
  customer-visible addresses stay `sales@de-linker.com`. Owner should be told.
  - The planned production proof is not quite right:
    - The sunriver inbox shares its domain with the new envelope, so a pass
  there is biased and doesn't stand in for a customer's mailbox.
  857558227@qq.com is the representative external receiver.
  chester@de-linker.com (Tencent-hosted, spoofed own-domain From) is the hardest
  case.
    - The 2026-09-30 inbox control changed From and envelope together, so it
  isolates neither.
    - The before and after messages should be the same mail type with the same
  body, sent to all three permitted mailboxes, with `Authentication-Results`
  recorded (not just the folder). One message each is weak evidence. If qq and
  chester@ still go to junk, the fallback in ticket step 3 (From on the sunriver
  domain) applies.

⏺ Agent "Spec review of PR 291" finished · 1m 36s

› Message from general-purpose
  **Standards review of PR #291 (`origin/main...pr291`, commits 620cf4ae,
  86ca9d4d, 2a06c6dc)**

  **Hard violations:** none found.

  **Checks you asked for**

  - **`phpmailer_init` and `Sender`: correct and safe.**
    - `Options::envelope_sender()` runs `sanitize_email` and then `is_email`.
  Anything else falls back to `DEFAULT_ENVELOPE_SENDER`: an empty value, an
  invalid address, or a non-string (the `text()` helper returns `''`).
    - `EnvelopeSender::apply` assigns only `$mailer->Sender`. It does not touch
  From, FromName or Reply-To. The unit test asserts this, and the integration
  test checks the delivered MIME.
    - The hook is registered unconditionally in `Mail/Module::register()`.
  - **Outbox retries: covered.** `Outbox::attempt()` calls `wp_mail()` again
  with the `delivering` flag set, so `pre_wp_mail` passes through and
  `phpmailer_init` fires. The setting is read again on every delivery.
  `Integration/EnvelopeSenderTest` proves this through `do_action( Outbox::HOOK,
  $job_id )` after the option is changed between queueing and delivery.
  - **.po churn: no German translation lost or changed.** I parsed the
  msgid→msgstr pairs before and after: 831 entries before, 834 after. Nothing
  was removed and no existing msgstr changed. The only difference in the header
  is `POT-Creation-Date`. Three entries were added (Mail delivery, Envelope
  sender, and the help text), in Sie form. The rest of the churn is `#:`
  reference lines (532) and reordering. The .mo contains the new strings
  (checked with msgunfmt).
    - About 48 REST-schema msgids already had empty msgstr on main and are still
  empty. This PR did not cause that.
  - **Addresses and secrets:** only allowed tester addresses,
  `sales@de-linker.com` (the documented public address) and `*@example.test`
  appear. No tokens or keys. No legacy identifiers (`tv_b2b_`, `Linker_AI_`, ADR
  or Law references).

  **Judgement calls (smells)**

  1. **Stale comment.** `Health/Monitor.php:20` still names
  `(Options::notify_from())`, but the code now calls `notify_sender()`.
  2. **Coupled defaults that mean different things.** `DEFAULT_ENVELOPE_SENDER =
  self::DEFAULT_NOTIFY_FROM;` ties an SPF-driven default to the owner's
  internal-recipient ruling. If one changes, the other moves silently. The spec
  accepts this on purpose ("共用同一来源"), but the two values have different
  sources of truth.
  3. **Primitive Obsession / Data Clump.** `notify_sender(): array{name: string,
  email: string}` is a string-keyed array read through `['name']` and
  `['email']` at five call sites.
  4. **Middle Man.** `notify_from()` stays public, but production code now only
  calls it from inside `Options`. Two public accessors for one fact.
  5. **Repeated Switches and a long test.** `Integration/EnvelopeSenderTest`
  branches on `'health' === $id` four times inside a test method of about 115
  lines.
  6. **Scope.** The `sanitize_prefix` trim of leading and trailing `-` in
  `SettingsTab.php:298` is unrelated to the envelope sender. It is permissible
  under the "本轮发现本轮修" rule, and it is logged in the spec change record.
  7. **Silent fallback.** An invalid saved envelope address is kept in the
  option and quietly replaced with the default at send time. Staff get no
  warning on save.

  **Number sourcing (§4):** the new constant has its SPF evidence comment. No
  new unsourced thresholds in production code.

  **Gap in the PR's own record:** production header, SPF and inbox evidence is
  still marked pending (`docs/acceptance/2026-10-18/288/review.md`).

⏺ Here's where everything stands.

  PR #290 (#246, open inquiries): changes requested; Codex is fixing them in the
  same thread.
  - No rate limit (the serious one): the request door limits nothing beyond its
    bot check, unlike the contact form, so a script could use the site to send
    mail to any address and burn the shared Hostinger quota of 100 mails a day.
    It will use the same per-visitor limit as the contact form.
  - Missing restricted reason: restricted accounts no longer see their reason on
    the site. It goes back above the form, and the form stays usable.
  - Leftovers: unused code, unsourced colours and sizes in the CSS, one
    untranslated label, and half-finished merging of the contact and inquiry
    records.
  - Pixel gate: the 57 differing pixels on the contact form are edge smoothing
    from comparing captures taken at different times. It still has to reach 0,
    so both sides get recaptured together. The contact page also differs in two
    photo bands; Codex will compare against a build of main without this change
    to find out whether this PR or the local copy causes it.

  #288 (mail envelope sender): it can't work on this host.
  - I sent the same test mail from the live site before and after the change, to
    the three allowed mailboxes.
  - Hostinger's mail program replaces the envelope sender with its own server
    address (noreply@srv1798.main-hosting.eu) whatever the site sets. SPF
    already passes today.
  - Sunriver puts mail from de-linker.com in junk either way. The From domain is
    the likely cause, since a test sent From sunriver last month reached the
    inbox. QQ put both versions in the inbox.
  - So PR #291 is being cut back: the envelope setting comes out and the useful
    side fixes stay.

  What fixes the internal-mail junk problem is #286, which sends internal

› Message from general-purpose
  **Standards review: PR #292 (36bb864c)**

  **Hard violations:** none found.
  - **Migration** (`log.py`): `approved INTEGER` is added through the existing
  `ADDED_COLUMNS` loop, which checks `PRAGMA table_info` before each `ALTER`.
  Running it twice is safe, existing rows are kept, and old rows get NULL, which
  `bool(row["approved"])` reads as `False`. The read-only worker against a log
  that hasn't been upgraded gets `NULL AS approved`.
  `test_an_old_log_gains_the_columns_and_keeps_its_rows` and `test_turn.py`
  cover it.
  - **Fixture** (`production-flags.json`): no emails, no secrets, no customer
  data. It holds synthetic conversation ids (9027/9033/9036/9051), product and
  manual data, URLs of our own hosted PDFs, and the one-word question "saj".
  `sales@de-linker.com` appears only in the rubric prose, where it is the
  address customers are meant to see.
  - **Model routing (AGENTS.md §5):** no channel changes. The spec text names
  only Jev via /systemone and the official deepseek-flash API.
  - **Legacy vocabulary:** none.
  - **Numbers:** the only new figures are in the spec doc (0.5, 118/118, 0.74,
  0.80), and each comes with its calibration source. Nothing new needs a source
  comment in code.

  **Judgement calls / smells:**
  1. **Duplicated Code / Shotgun Surgery** (`rubric.py` `BETTER_PICK`): it
  restates the remit in its own words ("answers from the hosted documents,
  warranty terms and installation requirements included, are within it; …")
  instead of interpolating `{REMIT}` as `APPROPRIATE` does. Its paraphrase also
  leaves out the new price rule. The spec now says the remit is "appropriate 与
  better_pick 共用", which the code doesn't do. Every future remit change has to
  be made in two places.
  2. **Behaviour gap, under "本轮发现本轮修" (AGENTS.md §5)** (`schema.py` and
  `app.py` `approved=body.user.partner`): the site `Proxy.php:262` deliberately
  never sends `partner`, so every logged turn stays `approved=False` until #242
  ships. But `ProductCommerce::of` already sets `price_visible`/`price` per
  visitor. If the abilities run as the signed-in user, every priced answer to an
  approved partner will now be flagged `appropriate = no`, the same kind of
  false flag this ticket fixes. Either confirm prices can't reach the turn
  before #242, or let the remit/item rely on the tool's own `price_visible`.
  3. **Primitive Obsession / fragile string surgery** (`quality/turns.py`): `sql
  = sql.replace(f", {column}", f", NULL AS {column}", 1)` patches the SQL text
  and relies on the first match landing in the column list. It works with
  today's `COLUMNS`, but it's brittle.
  4. **Duplicated knowledge** (`quality/turns.py`): `LATER_COLUMNS =
  ("question", "page", "approved")` mirrors `log.ADDED_COLUMNS` by hand; its own
  comment says so. It could be derived from `log.ADDED_COLUMNS` in the same
  package.
  5. **Mysterious Name / inconsistent naming:** one concept has three names,
  `user.partner` → `approved` (log and `LoggedTurn`) → `account.approved` (judge
  state). It is documented, but you have to translate at every step.
  6. **Brittle tests** (`test_quality.py`
  `test_the_remit_keeps_document_answers…` and
  `test_a_price_is_judged_against_the_account`): they split the prompt prose
  into sentences with `_sentence` and look for substrings. Any rewording breaks
  them even when the behaviour is the same.
  7. **Positive:** renaming `_bare_numbers` → `bare_numbers` and deleting
  citations.py's own `TOKEN`/`NUMBER` rules removes real duplication. The `re`
  import in `citations.py` is still used by `_occurs`.

› Message from general-purpose
  **Spec review, PR #292 (36bb864c).** Overall the PR matches the spec. I found
  no blocking issues.

  **(a) Missing or partial**
  - The spec asks for a threshold check after the rubric change. The #258
  comment says "改动 rubric 要重新校准阈值". 33-judge-bakeoff.md now records the
  results ("Jev 用新 remit 跑校准集 118 条问答，appropriate 118/118… 线上 20
  轮两次重判"). The run artifacts are not committed, though. The PR says the
  inputs were in a local scratch copy. So the evidence is a claim nobody can
  re-run. It counts as partial, not missing.

  **(b) Scope creep**
  - None. The `UserContext.partner` description, the `approved` log column and
  the rename of `_bare_numbers` to `bare_numbers` are all needed to carry the
  flag and to have one shared number rule. PR #292 does not send `partner` from
  the shell, which correctly leaves that to #242.

  **(c) Looks implemented, but questionable**
  1. **The "service commitments" ban is kept with nothing to support it.** REMIT
  still says it "never gives… service commitments". The owner requires a
  promise: 2026-10-09 "承诺一个工作日内回复询盘", and 13-genie.md 目标 5/6
  "承诺一个工作日内回复". A Genie answer that makes this mandated promise can be
  judged out of remit. The wording was there before this PR, but the remit was
  rewritten here and the ban kept. It should be removed or narrowed to delivery
  and service dates.
  2. **Stock wording is narrower than the 2026-10-11 ruling.** REMIT says "It
  may say that a product is in stock". The ruling allows both statuses
  ("对客户只说状态（有货/暂时无货）"), so "temporarily out of stock" is not
  covered. This is minor and also pre-existing.
  3. **The spec text describes the number rule too narrowly.** The new spec line
  calls model codes "大写字母与数字以连字符或斜杠相连". The shared rule also
  counts every whole word `figures.is_code` reads as a code, including
  lower-case and single-segment codes like GW10K. The code is right and the doc
  understates it.

  **Answers to the three questions**
  1. **Remit wording is faithful to the rulings.** The new REMIT keeps warranty
  terms, installation requirements and clearances in remit when answered from
  documents. It sends to sales@ "making a warranty claim, booking installation
  or commissioning, delivery, shipping terms and payment terms" and company
  questions. That fits 2026-10-05 ("公司、服务、物流类问题一句话转团队") and
  目标 1 ("公司、物流、售后"). Stock quantities and Incoterms stay banned
  (2026-10-02, 2026-10-11). The only rule with no ruling behind it is item c1.
  2. **The price rule is correct for #242 and 目标 8**
  ("已批准看净价，访客、待审、限制账号看不到"). True allows prices, false or
  missing means no price, and the default is the stricter reading. `partner` is
  documented as an approved partner account, matching
  `CommerceState::visitor_sees_prices()`, where pending and restricted accounts
  read the site like guests. Rejected accounts are not named but fall under
  false. Prices for rfq_only products stay safe, because the remit only allows
  prices that `reference` actually returned. #242's request
  ("补一条质检测试覆盖这两种情况") is met by
  `test_a_price_is_judged_against_the_account`. One thing to carry over: when
  #242 sets `partner`, it must use `Approval::sees_prices`, not `logged_in`.
  3. **The judge stays post-hoc.** In `app.py` the only change passes
  `approved=` into `TurnLog.record`. The `ground.py` change is a rename with the
  same behaviour. Nothing in the answer path changes, which matches #258
  "只做事后质检".

› Message from general-purpose
  **Standards review: PR #293 (#245)**

  I found no hard violations. The PR uses the agreed channels:
  - The card layout is an LC fragment
  (`content/livecanvas/account/admin-access.html`).
  - Styles live only in the WindPress volume (`content/windpress/auth.css`).
  There is no inline CSS and no plugin stylesheet.
  - The PHP shortcode `field()` returns only escaped values (`esc_html`,
  `esc_url`).
  - `templates/myaccount/admin-access.php` is a one-line pass-through, the same
  pattern as the existing `auth/pending.php`.

  Security is fine:
  - Sign-out uses WordPress's own nonce-protected `wp_logout_url( wp_login_url(
  admin_url() ) )`, with a fixed return address.
  - `destination()` builds the redirect from the account page ID and never reads
  a request parameter, so it cannot be used as an open redirect. It removes
  itself after one use, and Woo still calls `wp_safe_redirect`.
  - `$_GET` and `$_COOKIE` are only compared strictly against fixed strings.

  Other checks: no legacy identifiers, and the only real email is
  857558227@qq.com, which is allowed. The fixture password is a throwaway for
  local tests only. `.po`, `.pot` and `.mo` were updated in EN and DE, in Sie
  form.

  **Judgement calls**
  1. **Numbers need a source comment (AGENTS.md §4,
  "每个数字与阈值必须有来源注释").** `AdminAccessNotice.php:19` has `add_filter(
  'woocommerce_prevent_admin_access', …, PHP_INT_MAX )` with no comment in the
  code; the reason is only in the spec. AGENTS.md lists PHP_INT_MAX priority as
  a known past deviation, so this one should say why in the code. Priority `50`
  on `wc_get_template` is explained only indirectly ("Takes precedence over the
  pending-review card", which uses 40).
  2. **Duplicated Code / Shotgun Surgery.** `requested()` copies Woo's staff
  capability list:
     `! current_user_can( 'edit_posts' ) && ! current_user_can(
  'manage_woocommerce' ) … 'view_admin_dashboard'`
     If Woo changes `WC_Admin::prevent_admin_access`, this page will disagree
  with Woo's actual decision.
  3. **Primitive Obsession / Duplicated Code.** `'de' ===
  $_COOKIE['pll_language'] ? 'de' : 'en'` hard-codes the languages and
  Polylang's cookie name. The `PLL_COOKIE` constant and the `linker-i18n`
  `Languages` helper already exist for this.
  4. **Shotgun Surgery (existing, made worse).** In
  `ProductShortcodes.php:2021/2028`, `'admin-access'` had to be added to two
  separate key lists. The comment above them, which explains each kept key, was
  not updated.
  5. **Missing unit test (§4: every module has tests).** `AdminAccessNotice` has
  e2e coverage only. Its siblings `PendingGate` and `AuthTemplates` have unit
  tests. `destination()` (cookie and twin-page fallback) and the `field()`
  switch have no PHPUnit coverage.
  6. **German copy (copy-review rule).** "Sie sind mit %s als Kundenkonto
  angemeldet" equates the person with an account; "mit Ihrem Kundenkonto %s"
  reads better. "als Mitarbeitende anmelden" is a plural or feminine form used
  for one person. The copy review was done by the implementing session itself,
  so an independent reviewer should check the German.
  7. **Minor `.po` ordering.** The `#.` translator comment comes after `#:`;
  gettext tools put it first. This suggests the `.po` was edited by hand rather
  than regenerated.

  **Smells I checked and rejected:** The `field()` switch is a single
  key-to-value map, not a repeated switch. `Tokens::read` is used for reading
  HTML, but that is an existing pattern.

  - "User Switching 不受影响": tested switching to the customer and back. The

  - "说明页 EN/DE, 任何审批状态": works. Within each language/width, the 8 state

  ticket did not ask for.

  pending-gate card (same shell, card, primary and ghost buttons). Only three
  scoped CSS rules were added. pixel-parity.json compares the existing sign-in

  ("每张非基础票一份规格，文件名 = 票号"), like 184-account-chain.md. It should
  probably link to 184-account-chain.md.

✻ Churned for 1m 39s · done 3:16 AM

› Message from general-purpose
  **Standards review of PR #294 (#229), code hunks**

  **Hard violations:** none found. Styles go only into the WindPress volume
  (`content/windpress/chrome.css`), with no PHP, inline style, LC/PHP layout
  branch or second CSS channel (AGENTS.md §4, §5 "PHP 不解决设计问题"). The
  diff's added lines contain no `tv_b2b_`, `Linker_AI_`, `dlk-`, Law or ADR
  text. The DE visual change is recorded in
  `docs/acceptance/2026-10-18/229/change-ledger.md`, and EN shows 0 diff pixels
  at 1280/1440/1920/390 with no mask (§5 pixel rule).

  **Judgement calls**

  1. **chrome.css:49–53. The 5px source comment is incomplete (§4
  "每个数字与阈值必须有来源注释").** The comment explains 5px only as "returns
  108px … to fit beside the contact button." The README shows a second
  constraint that actually decided the value: at 4px, DE unfolds at 1280 while
  EN stays folded. So 5px is a two-sided bound tied to the runtime `fit()`
  measurement in `header-menu.js`, and it depends on font metrics. The comment
  should name both bounds: DE must fit at 1440, and DE must stay folded at 1280
  like EN. That coupling is a mild **Shotgun Surgery** risk: changing a label,
  the font or `fit()` silently moves the window. The e2e matrix does catch it,
  which helps.
  2. **Language selector.** `html[lang^="de"]` matches the existing JS check
  (`document.documentElement.lang.startsWith('de')`), so both read one signal.
  It cannot match EN `en-*`, and the EN 0-pixel results confirm it doesn't leak.
  Robust enough; I see no reason to prefer a Polylang body class.
  3. **header-menu.js:80–87, the focus fix.** It is minimal and correct: an
  `aria-expanded` guard plus a one-shot `focused` flag. Both new jsdom tests
  cover the two races (Escape before the delayed callback runs; the fallback
  timer firing after `transitionend` and a later arrow), and they were shown red
  then green. Smell (mild, pre-existing pattern): **Primitive Obsession /
  Duplicated Code.** `button.getAttribute('aria-expanded') !== 'true'` now
  appears three times in the closure (`wasClosed`, the guard, the click
  handler). A local `isOpen()` would remove the repetition. Separately, the
  keydown block is indented one tab too deep, which is pre-existing and not
  worth blocking on.
  4. **tests/e2e/specs/header-parity.spec.ts.**
     - **Duplicated Code:** the key list
  `['products','solutions','ai','partners','about']` is written out twice in the
  1440 and 390 branches. Hoist it to one constant.
     - **Unsourced numbers (§4):** `timeout: 45_000` and the viewport `height:
  900` have no source. The comment explains why there is a timeout but not where
  45 s comes from.
     - The tests check rendered boxes rather than class names, and they repeat
  cleanly (14/14 twice, no DB writes). Good.
  5. **Pixel tooling location.** `docs/acceptance/2026-10-18/229/pixels.py` and
  `capture.sh` sit beside `249/pixel/pixdiff.py`, which does a similar job, and
  `capture.sh` reaches into another ticket's folder for `249/pixel/shoot.mjs`.
  That is **Duplicated Code** across acceptance folders, and cross-ticket path
  coupling makes reruns fragile. The repeatable pixel gate belongs under
  `tests/` or one shared script. This was already found this round, so §5
  "本轮发现本轮修" applies to it.
  6. **Spec doc item 9 is fine as written.** Its reasoning ("实测英语在 1280
  已因运行时量宽折叠") is properly evidenced.

  Verdict: mergeable. Before merging, fix items 1 and 4 (source comments and the
  hoisted list). Item 5 needs either a fix or a recorded decision.

› Message from general-purpose
  **Spec review, PR #294 (#229): one requirement is only partly met, there is no
  scope creep, and one part works but could be done better.**

  I measured this myself with Playwright against local c229 (`127.0.0.1:9099`)
  and live. I swept the `data-tv-nav-folded` state from 1276 to 1940px, in 1px
  steps up to 1296 and 10px steps above that.

  **(a) Missing or partial**

  1. **German folds at a different width from English.** The decision says "keep
  the full desktop nav plus the contact button at the same breakpoints as
  English", and the ticket says "同一断点才折叠".
     - English is folded at 1280–1281 and shows the full row from **1282**.
     - German at 5px is folded up to 1287 and shows the full row from **1288**.
     - So from **1282 to 1287px** English shows the full row and German shows
  the hamburger. Above 1288 both match: both compact, both unfolded at 1300,
  1366, 1400 and on to 1940.
     - The PR does not mention this window. The e2e test checks only
  1280/1440/1920/390. The README even records that 4px "让德语 1280 提前展开",
  so it is known that the boundary depends on the padding value, but the actual
  boundary was never measured.
     - No padding value lines up exactly. 4.5px gives German 1281 against
  English 1282, and 4.75px gives 1285.
     - Fix: either record the 6px gap in the change ledger as a known
  difference, or tune the padding (4.5px gets within 1px) and add a boundary
  sweep test.

  **(b) Scope creep: none**

  The focus fix is in scope. AGENTS.md §5 says "本轮发现的问题本轮修". It is in
  the same header script and was found during the required per-state check. It
  was written test-first (both tests failed, then passed) and does not touch
  `fit()`.

  **(c) Works but could be done better**

  2. **German spacing is a uniform 5px, but it only has to be that tight near
  1288px.** The map #220 goal is "EN/DE headers identical".
     - The nav frame stops growing at 1392px.
     - Probe at 1440/1920: German still fits at **12px** and folds only at 14px.
  At 1366 it fits at 10px; at 1300 only 5px works.
     - So German at 1440/1920, the widths the ticket screenshots, is visibly
  tighter than English (5px against 14px; see `pixels/de-1440-headers.png` next
  to `home-1440-headers.png`), when 12px would look nearly the same as English.
     - Shrinking English to the same spacing is not an option: it breaks the
  English pixel-zero rule, so a German-only rule is the right approach.
     - The better reading is to use the largest padding that fits at each width,
  for example 5px at 1280–1365, 10px at 1366–1439 and 12px from 1440. That
  means a few extra German media rules in `chrome.css` and no change to `fit()`.
     - The ledger would also need to say why German cannot reach 14px at
  1440/1920 even after that.

  **Confirmed met**

  - English shows 0 unmasked pixel differences at all four widths
  (`results.json`).
  - German's pixel changes stay in the header area, y=21–67.
  - No wording was changed. `copy-review.md` records that all existing labels
  were kept, which is consistent.
  - CSS was tried first and `fit()` is unchanged.
  - The change ledger has before and after screenshots.
  - The 1280 handling (both languages folded, because live English already folds
  there) is the right reading. The PR explains it.

  **Verdict:** request changes on items 1 and 2. Both are small CSS changes plus
  updates to the ledger and tests.

  My probe scripts are in `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-chesterjohn-Documents-
  de-linker/483dcdbc-1776-4534-a035-3a4a72690ee0/scratchpad/` (`sweep.mjs` and
  `padprobe.mjs`).

  All review round 1 items for PR #292 are fixed in commit b82e6142 on

  **Standards**
  1. **Shared remit:** `BETTER_PICK` now embeds `{REMIT}` word for word, as
  `APPROPRIATE` does; a test checks both.
  2. **Price has two gates:** a price is in remit only when this turn's tool
  returned it as visible (`price_visible`) and `account.approved` is true.
     - `REMIT` states the rule, and `quality/citations.py` now enforces it
  deterministically. A visible price shown to an account that is not approved is
  flagged as "states the price of product N, and the account is not approved".
  A price the tool did not return fails as an unbacked number, approved or not.
     - Tests cover visible+approved (passes), visible+not approved (flagged) and
  not returned+approved (flagged).
     - **Who the abilities run as today:** anonymously (`abilities.py`), so
  `price_visible` is false on every turn. The proxy also sends no `partner`, so
  every turn logs as not approved. Both gates are closed until #242.
     - Spec 33 records this, and the #242 note that the proxy must use
  `Approval::sees_prices` / `visitor_sees_prices()`, not `logged_in`.
  3. **SELECT list:** `quality/turns.py` now builds it from the `COLUMNS` tuple
  (`select_list`) instead of string replacement.
  4. **Later columns:** `LATER_COLUMNS` is now derived from `log.ADDED_COLUMNS`;
  a test covers it.

  **Spec**
  - **c1:** the remit no longer bans "service commitments" as a whole. It still
  bans delivery times or dates, service or appointment dates, Incoterms and any
  commitment the documents do not make. It explicitly allows the one-working-day
  reply promise (test added).
  - **c2:** the remit allows "in stock or temporarily out of stock" and still
  bans quantities (test added).
  - **c3:** spec 33 describes the shared number rule in the order the code
  applies it: `_MODEL_CODE`, then `figures.is_code` per word (single-segment
  codes like GW10K count; "5kWh" does not), then pure numbers.
  - **Calibration artefact:** committed in
  `docs/research/2026-10-18-judge-remit/`.
    - Re-run instructions: `README.md`.

  `rejudge-round1.json` (the two earlier re-judgings).

✻ Worked for 1m 9s · done 3:44 AM

› Message from general-purpose
  #234 (Genie reads the current page) is finished. PR #295 is open, non-draft
  and not merged: https://github.com/AssassinFlash/de-linker/pull/295. CI is
  green (`check` and `mu-smoke` both passed), and I commented "PR #295 待评审"
  on #234. The branch `feat/234-page-context` is rebased on origin/main, which
  now includes #292, and `just check` and `just test` both pass locally.

  **What it does**
  - **Page context:** every turn now carries the page's type, URL, title and IDs
  (product, post, category, draft). The shell builds it in one place,
  `plugins/linker-tools/includes/Genie/PageContext.php`. It covers the eight
  page types plus `other`.
    - On a German product page, `product_id` is still the English product, so
  "this one" resolves correctly.
    - The proxy keeps only the known keys of what the browser sends, and it
  reads `draft_ids` from the visitor's own draft rather than trusting the
  browser.
  - **Schema change is additive only:** `PageType` gains `home`, `solution`,
  `blog` and `faq`; `PageContext` gains `url`, `title`, `post_id` and
  `category_id`. Nothing existing was changed, which keeps the merge clean for
  #242 and #236.
  - **Page content source:** I did not use WP REST. On 2026-10-18 I measured
  that its FAQ content is an old body with unrendered shortcodes, while visitors
  see 244 FAQs rendered by a LiveCanvas template, and the shop, categories,
  draft, account and blog index have no REST body at all.
    - Instead, `services/genie/src/linker_genie/page.py` (tool
  `linker/read-page`) fetches the page anonymously. It takes only the URL's path
  on `GENIE_WP_URL`, follows redirects only on the same site, and reads the
  text in `<main>`.
    - Long pages are trimmed to about 12,000 characters around the question.
  Figures on the page are cited as `{fact:page.nK}`, the same way document
  figures are.
    - The draft is read as its products plus inverter–battery pairing checks; a
  signed-in account is read through inquiry-status.
    - This is written into spec 13 under "页面感知", and spec 10 notes it.
  - **When the page is read:** Jev's "is this about the page" answer decides.
  The page read is added before the route's own calls, within the 4-call limit.
  It is skipped for "talk to a person", company questions and inquiry status.
  When Jev is unsure, the model can call the page tool itself.
  - **Opening suggestions now change per page:** on the four page types that
  have the #249 preview card, the dock shows the card's questions, which name
  the product or category. The new suggestions for the other pages went through
  an independent adversarial copy review, with German written separately in the
  `.po` file. Two system-prompt sentences (on the page itself, and on what
  happens after the draft, using "business day" / "Arbeitstag") were reviewed
  too.
  - **#249 preview card** now takes its page type and name from this page
  context; which pages show the card is unchanged.

  **Completion criteria**

  | Criterion | Status and evidence |
  |---|---|
  | Two questions per page type pass the eval, EN and DE | Done. 32/32 in the
  final run (`docs/acceptance/2026-10-18/234/eval/run-20261018T033006.json`). I
  read every answer: 32 correct, 0 wrong. The previous run scored 31/32 because
  the check only read answer text, while the German "check my draft" answer
  named the products as cards; I widened the check to accept cards. |
  | "What does this page mean by…" questions | Done. Covered on the home,
  product, category, solution and blog pages; each answer came from the page and
  cites it. |
  | "This one" on a product page without retyping the model | Done. Jev scored
  0.97/0.98 that it was about the page, the page's product became the subject,
  and Genie answered 4 MPPTs. |
  | Opening suggestions change with the page | Done.
  `tests/e2e/specs/genie-page.spec.ts` passes 48/48 (8 page types × EN/DE × open
  at 1440, open at 390, real answer at 1440). Screenshots are in
  `docs/acceptance/2026-10-18/234/screens/`. |
  | Copy review | Done. `docs/acceptance/2026-10-18/234/copy-review.md`, with

✻ Cogitated for 45s · done 3:48 AM

› Message from general-purpose
  **Standards review of PR #295 (ticket #234)**

  I read the code diff only, not the bulk under docs/acceptance. I found no
  legacy identifiers (`tv_b2b_`, `Linker_AI_`, `dlk-`, ADR). The model routing
  is unchanged: the page read makes no model call, and Jev only gets a `title`
  added to its state. The proxy whitelist is sound: it uses `sanitize()`, casts
  the IDs to int, takes `draft_ids` from the server-side cart only, and drops
  any `draft_ids` the browser sends.

  **Hard issues (security brief and AGENTS.md)**

  1. **No size limit or content-type check on the fetch** (`page.py` `_html`).
  `response.text` buffers the whole body and `HTMLParser` then runs over all of
  it. `page_path` does not exclude `/wp-content/`. So a browser can send
  `url=…/wp-content/uploads/<datasheet>.pdf` with `type:"other"`, and the
  service downloads and parses a large binary on the same host. A streamed byte
  cap and an `text/html` check are missing. The brief asked for this explicitly.
  2. **The fetch is not fully anonymous.** One shared `httpx.Client` keeps a
  cookie jar across every customer's reads. Any `Set-Cookie` the site sends back
  is replayed on later reads, for example Polylang's `pll_language`, which can
  change how `/` redirects. The client needs `cookies=None` handling or a jar
  cleared per request.
  3. **Redirects are checked on `netloc` only, not the scheme.** It is safe in
  effect, because only `parts.path` is re-fetched through `base_url`. But it
  does not match the stated scheme+host+port rule. It is worth a one-line
  comment or an explicit check.
  4. **The URL can exceed the length limit after truncation** (`PageContext.php`
  `sanitize`). It runs `esc_url_raw( mb_substr( …, 0, URL_LENGTH ) )`, and
  percent-encoding can push the result past 2048 characters. The service then
  returns 422 for the whole turn. It needs to truncate after escaping.
  5. **Numbers without a source comment** (AGENTS.md §4: every number and
  threshold needs a source comment):
     - `DRAFT_ITEMS = 50`, `MAX_REDIRECTS = 3`, `PASSAGE_CHARS = 700` and
  `LEAD_CHARS = 1_500` have reasons but no source.
     - `_QUOTED {3,80}`, `len(word) >= 3` and `capture_pages.py timeout=30.0`
  have no comment at all.
     - The `schema.py` `max_length=2048/300` limits, which the PHP code mirrors,
  have no source either.

  **Judgement calls**

  - **Prompt injection.** Page text goes in as "the site's own copy". Neither
  `system.md` nor `PAGE_HINT` says to treat it as data and never as
  instructions. Citations still hold, because figures are hydrated server-side
  from `tagged.figures`. The risk is real only if `<main>` holds text visitors
  write, such as product reviews or comments.
  - **Vocabulary** (AGENTS.md §3, 不说 catalogue): the comments in `Entries.php`
  ("The catalogue page (/products/)") and `Preview.php` ("The catalogue
  (/products/)") use the banned word.
  - **Turn log size.** `page.record` stores the full payload, up to about 12k
  characters of text, in `tool_calls` on every turn.

  **Code smells**

  - **Duplicated code.** `route.py` has `READ_PAGE = "linker/read-page"`, which
  copies `page.NAME`. It also has `TURN_CALLS = 4`, which copies
  `agent.MAX_TOOL_CALLS` (its comment says so). `facts.py` uses
  `name.endswith("read-page")` instead of `page.NAME`.
  - **Shotgun surgery.** The page citation rule (`page` ref, `{fact:page.nK}`)
  is written out four times: in `system.md` EN and DE, `route.PAGE_HINT`,
  `agent._page_lines` and `_user_prompt`.
  - **Repeated switches.** The page-type list lives in `PageContext::TYPES`,
  `schema.PageType` and `jev._PAGE_TYPES`, and is switched on in
  `Entries::prompts` and `Preview::card_page`. Across languages some of this is
  unavoidable.
  - **Long parameter list.** `new Proxy( $conversations, null, null, null,
  $page_context )` passes three positional nulls.
  - **Feature envy.** `Preview` calls `$this->pages->plain()`. The
  title-cleaning helper moved onto `PageContext`, which does not own that
  concern.

⏺ The Standards axis for #295 is in. It found real fetch-safety gaps:
  - No size or type limit: a browser could point the page read at a large PDF in
    uploads, and the service would download and parse it whole.
  - Shared cookie jar: one cookie store is reused across every customer's reads,
    so a cookie set for one visitor carries into the next.
  - Missing source comments on several numbers.

  I'll put this together with the Spec axis when it arrives.

  **Spec review, PR #295 (#234).** Read: the diff, issue/PR, the kickoff #234
  section, spec 13 (目标 and 页面感知), spec 10, all 32 answers in
  run-20261018T033006.json, copy-review.md, and 3 screenshots.

  **(a) Missing or partial**
  1. **Prices differ by viewer.** Spec: "服务端解析成该页的真实内容". The page
  is read as an anonymous visitor. The product fixture text says "Net price
  after partner sign-in", while an approved partner sees the net price on the
  page. Neither PAGE_HINT nor the system prompt says that prices never come from
  page text. So a signed-in partner who asks "what's the price here?" can be
  told to sign in, which contradicts their own screen. Fix: drop the price/gate
  block in `page.py`, or add a prompt rule, and coordinate with #242.
  2. **Delivery figures from the page.** The same text "Germany delivery
  estimate: €39.00 to €90.00" is tagged `page.nK`, so the model can quote it.
  That clashes with the 2026-10-05 ruling
  (公司、服务、物流类问题一句话转团队邮箱) whenever Jev doesn't pick
  `company_logistics`.
  3. `page_path` drops the query string, so a filtered category or search page
  is read unfiltered. Minor.

  **(b) Scope creep**
  - `with_page` sets `no_handoff=True` on every about-page route. This isn't in
  spec 13 页面感知. Either document it or drop it.

  **(c) Implemented but wrong**
  - **Budget.** `calls = (*added, *kept)[:TURN_CALLS]` silently cuts the route's
  own trailing calls. Spec: "合计仍 ≤4 次调用" is met, but needed calls can be
  lost:
    - Draft page, documents question: since `subject.ids` now = draft_ids, you
  get read-page + 2× check-compatibility + search-knowledge, and both
  get-product calls are dropped.
    - Product page, documents route with 2 IDs: all 4 calls are used, so the
  model cannot do the prompt's "search again with its product_ids".
    - Neither drop is logged. The "this one" turn uses 3 calls (fine).
  - **Eval README is wrong about the draft.** It says the draft is
  GW110K-ETR-G10 ×2 + ESA + BAT, but the eval rows use `draft_ids [6528, 6520]`.
  The multi-pair truncation case isn't evaluated; only the e2e screenshot shows
  it.
  - "catalogue" appears in an `Entries.php` comment (AGENTS §3). Cosmetic.

  **Your questions**
  1. **Content source.** Fetching the rendered page is the right call: the REST
  evidence holds, it's native-first, and no WP code is needed.
     - **German:** DE turns fetch the DE URL (the URL comes from REQUEST_URI;
  the eval fetched `/de/produkt/…` and `/de/warenkorb/`).
     - **Signed-in pages:** handled correctly. The proxy reads the draft
  server-side via `wc_load_cart` and names its products through the Store API;
  the account page goes to inquiry-status.
     - **Latency:** an uncached LiveCanvas render takes 0.5–3.4 s and runs
  sequentially before the model, with a 10-minute cache. Eval turns took 9–17 s
  on product and category pages. Consider running it in parallel with the other
  route calls.
  2. **Tool budget.** See (c).
  3. **System-prompt sentence.** Consistent. "within a business day" /
  "innerhalb eines Arbeitstags" matches the commerce `.po`, and the one-day
  promise applies only to the reply. Status names match §3 exactly; no cart or
  basket wording. Guests are not pointed to an account.
  4. **Opening suggestions.** At least 2 per type, EN/DE, written in the right
  vocabulary. They went through copy review with an independent reviewer plus a
  live trial. DE uses questions in Sie form.

  **Evidence:** I judged all 32 answers correct and grounded. The "this one"
  turn needed no model name and gave 4 MPPTs in both EN and DE. Live production
  verification has not been done yet (planned for #237).

› Message from general-purpose
  ## #235 Genie: keep every conversation: PR #296 is ready for review

  PR #296 (https://github.com/AssassinFlash/de-linker/pull/296) is open, not a
  draft, and CI passed (check (8.3, 22, 3.12) and mu-smoke). It is rebased on
  the latest origin/main and is one commit, `da0441d1`. I commented "PR #296
  待评审" on #235 and stopped there: nothing merged or deployed, nothing written
  to production.

  **What it does:**
  - **A guest keeps every conversation.** Each browser gets a random key in an
  httponly cookie (`linker_genie_browser`). Every guest conversation post stores
  a hash of that key, so the list shows all of the browser's conversations,
  newest first by start date.
    - Both cookies last 90 days, the existing guest retention period
  (`Retention::RETENTION_DAYS`).
    - A guest from before this change keeps their single existing conversation
  when they open the next one.
  - **Signing in moves them all into the account.** This happens on both the
  normal login and on WooCommerce's automatic sign-in after registration (that
  path does not fire `wp_login`, so it needed its own hook). The account then
  shows them on any device.
  - **Clearing.** A conversation can be deleted from the list after an in-place
  confirm. It is removed with its thread, so it is no longer context for
  anything. Another visitor gets a 404.
  - **Email to yourself.** A signed-in customer gets the conversation at their
  account address; a guest types an address. The limit is 3 per connection per
  hour, the same as the contact form.
    - The email itself is a linker-commerce WooCommerce customer email,
  `Genie\ConversationEmail`, sent through the Outbox. linker-commerce switches
  off any WooCommerce email outside its own namespace, so it could not live in
  linker-tools; linker-tools asks for it on the filter
  `linker_tools_genie_send_conversation`.
    - Each send builds a fresh email object. Reusing one kept the first email's
  language, so a German email sent after an English one had the English subject.
  - **The rail stays open.** On a wide workspace (768 px and up), starting or
  switching a conversation no longer folds the sidebar. Under 768 px the sidebar
  is an overlay over the conversation, so it still folds after you pick one.
  The dock never folds on "new conversation".
  - **Spec and copy.** `docs/specs/13-genie.md` §C now says how the browser
  cookies relate to the server records, and has a changelog entry. All new copy
  went through an adversarial review by an independent agent; the record is in
  `copy-review.md`.

  **List order changed:** I switched the conversation list from last-modified to
  start date, because sign-in rewrites the modified date and shuffled the list.

✻ Sautéed for 15m 57s · done 4:19 AM

› Message from general-purpose
  **Standards review: PR #296 (da0441d1)**

  I found two hard violations, three security weaknesses that need a decision,
  and five smaller judgement calls.

  **Hard violations**
  1. **German JS strings were added by hand, so the .po no longer matches the
  JSON.** All 19 new Genie strings were typed straight into `plugins/linker-tool
  s/languages/linker-tools-de_DE_formal-linker-tools-genie.json`. They are
  missing from `linker-tools-de_DE_formal.po`, which already holds the older
  Genie strings ("New conversation", "Privacy Policy"). This breaks AGENTS.md §4
  (`@wordpress/i18n` + `wp i18n make-json`): the next make-json run will drop
  every new string and German will fall back to English. The rule "EN/DE always
  delivered in sync" is broken as a result.
  2. **The guest self-email has no bot guard.** `ConversationMail.php` cites
  `Contact\Handler::LIMIT` as its precedent. But the contact handler also has a
  nonce and a honeypot ("website"), and this route has `permission_callback =>
  '__return_true'` with no guard at all. For a guest, the REST nonce proves
  nothing.

  **Security (judgement, but material)**
  - **Spam relay.** A guest writes the user messages themselves, then mails the
  conversation to any address, sent from sales@de-linker.com. The only limit is
  3 per hour per `REMOTE_ADDR`, and IPv6 or proxy rotation gets around it.
  Nothing caps sends globally against the shared 100/24h quota. The Outbox
  queues instead of refusing, so abuse delays inquiry notifications and puts the
  one-business-day reply promise at risk.
  - **Race in the limit.** `recent()` is checked before `apply_filters` and
  `count()` runs after it, so parallel requests can all get past the limit.
  - **Browser key is never rotated.** `claim_for()` deletes the hash mark but
  keeps the cookie. On a shared browser, guest conversations made after a logout
  are claimed by whoever signs in next. `set_logged_in_cookie` also fires on
  User Switching, which runs on production.
  - **Sound:** `random_bytes(16)`, a sha256-only hash, `hash_equals`, the cookie
  flags (httponly, Secure via `is_ssl()`, Lax), `delete()` (404 via `may_use`,
  force delete removes the thread meta), `esc_html`/`nl2br` on all content in
  the template, and the filter returning a 503 "unavailable" when
  linker-commerce is inactive.

  **Smells (judgement)**
  - **Speculative Generality:** `title` is checked in `Conversation::from_array`
  and passed through the filter, but the template never uses it.
  - **Duplicated Code:**
    - The rate limiter (`limit_key`/`recent`/`count`) is copied from
  `Contact\Handler`.
    - The cookie dispatch arguments (`defined('COOKIEPATH') ? … , COOKIE_DOMAIN
  …, is_ssl()`) are repeated in `set_cookie`, `clear_cookie` and
  `ensure_browser_key`.
    - Role normalisation is written in both `Thread::transcript` and
  `Conversation::from_array`.
  - **Primitive Obsession:** the six-value status string is repeated in two
  docblocks, in the `Proxy` `$codes` map, and again as a mapping from HTTP code
  back to status in `turn.ts`.
  - **Numbers without a source** (rule: every number has a source comment):
    - `config.ts` has `Number( data.retentionDays ) || 90`, a second 90 with no
  source.
    - The new `genie.css` rules use raw hex colours (`#8f8f8f`, `#ededed`,
  `#181818`) and px values with no source. They skip the "tokens with fallbacks"
  rule stated in the file header, and that header's claim that every rule comes
  from the prototype is no longer true.
  - **Already in main, not introduced here:** Genie styles live in a
  hand-written `genie.css` rather than going through WindPress (AGENTS.md §4).
  This PR adds about 167 more lines to that file.

  **Clean:** no old-system identifiers or numbers, PSR-4 one class per file,
  test mail stopped at `pre_wp_mail` and routing checked against the Outbox
  queue, `OVERLAY_WIDTH = 768` has its source, LIMIT/WINDOW cite their source,
  the commerce .po/.pot/.mo are updated, and the 90-day cookie lifetime is tied
  to `Retention::RETENTION_DAYS`.

⏺ The Standards axis for #296 is in. It found two hard problems:
  - German strings typed straight into the compiled file. The 19 new German
    Genie strings went directly into the compiled translation file and are
    missing from the translation source, so the next rebuild would wipe them.
  - No bot protection on guest self-email. The "email this conversation to
    yourself" option has no bot check for guests. Anyone could use it to send
    mail from sales@de-linker.com to any address, using up the shared daily mail
    quota.

  I'll combine these with the Spec axis when it arrives.

⏺ Agent "Standards review of PR 296" finished · 1m 52s

› Message from general-purpose
  **Spec review: PR #296 (#235).** Overall: substantially meets the spec. I
  found no blocker. Two findings are partial, two are wrong-ish, and there is no
  scope creep.

  **(a) Missing or partial**
  1. **The email loses the products.** A12 says "可把对话 / 摘要发到客户邮箱".
  `Thread::transcript()` sends only the text blocks, so the product cards are
  dropped. The DE screenshot `de-1440-6-email-message.png` shows the result: the
  answer says "Die konkreten Kennwerte und Preise stehen in den Produkten
  unten", and nothing appears below it. The cards are the core of an answer
  (goal 9: "产品卡带图"). Fix: at least list each product's name/model and link
  in the email, or drop the dangling reference.
  2. **The sender is not checked for this email.** The rule is that
  customer-visible sender stays sales@de-linker.com (AGENTS 2026-10-17).
  `ConversationEmail` inherits Woo's From and Reply-to, which is correct by
  construction. But `CustomerSenderTest` does not cover this email, and
  `GenieConversationEmailTest` does not assert From/Reply-to. One assertion
  would close this.

  **(b) Scope creep:** none. Delete and email are both required by goal 7:
  "可切换、可清除、可发到自己的邮箱".

  **(c) Implemented but questionable**
  3. **"自己邮箱" is really any mailbox.** A guest can type any address, with no
  confirmation. The email does say where it came from ("As requested on
  de-linker.com…"), and that is good. However, the body carries guest-typed
  text, sent from sales@de-linker.com. The only limit is 3 per IP per hour,
  while the host quota is "100 封/24 小时，四站共用" (§4). Someone rotating IPs
  could use up the quota that inquiry and approval mail depend on. The copy
  review accepts the risk as "the same as the contact form". I recommend adding
  a site-wide daily cap for this email type.
  4. **List order is by start date.** That satisfies "可切回", but users expect
  the most recently active conversation first: a conversation you go back to and
  continue stays buried. The PR also changes the order for signed-in customers,
  who were sorted by `modified` on main. Its stated reason is sound: naming and
  the sign-in claim rewrite `modified`. The right fix is a last-turn timestamp
  in meta, not `post_date`. Minor.

  **On your specific questions**
  - **(1) The httponly cookie is a faithful reading of "保存在本浏览器".**
  Conversations are bound to this browser, nothing is lost, and the browser key
  makes "登录后全部并入账户" possible. §C covers it clearly: what lives where,
  cookie lifetimes tied to `RETENTION_DAYS`, and what happens when cookies are
  cleared.
  - **(2) Clearing one conversation meets A9** ("清除"后不再作为上下文). The
  post and its thread are deleted, and the VPS turn log is not used as context.
  A9 does not ask for "clear all", so its absence is acceptable.
  - **(3) Wording is correct.** The email uses "Genie, Linker's AI assistant"
  and "Genie, dem KI-Assistenten von Linker", with no "Linker Genie". The DE is
  written separately, uses Sie, and the copy review is on file. Replies go to
  the From address, which is right.
  - **(5) Folding below 768 px is a reasonable reading of "新建不收起面板".**
  The kickoff complaint was the unconditional fold. At 390 px the overlay covers
  about two-thirds of the screen (`en-390-2-guest-three.png`), so leaving it
  open would hide the conversation just picked. The rule is written into §C.
  Note there is no 390 screenshot of "new conversation"; the evidence is the
  1440 shot plus vitest.

  **Evidence:** All completion criteria map to screenshots and tests. Test-mail
  discipline is kept (one email to tester, no Cc/Bcc, Outbox rows asserted).

⏺ Both axes for #296 are in. The Spec side is solid; the Standards side found
  real problems in the guest email path. Each item is decided:

› Message from general-purpose
  Every item from review round 1 on PR #295 is fixed: all Standards items,
  including the decided judgement calls, and Spec 1–7. CI is green (`check` and
  `mu-smoke`), and the branch is not merged.

  The fixes are in commit `9774e8ff` on `feat/234-page-context`, rebased on
  origin/main. `just check` and `just test` both pass. My reply is posted on the
  PR:
  https://github.com/AssassinFlash/de-linker/pull/295#issuecomment-6240557329. I
  used the tdd skill for fetch safety, price and delivery stripping, and the
  call budget: each test failed first, then passed after the fix.

  **Fetch safety and other Standards fixes:**
  - **Size and type:** the page is streamed and refused above 2,000,000 bytes;
  the largest live page (the German FAQ) is 718,375 bytes. Only `text/html`
  responses are read.
  - **Paths never requested:** `/wp-content`, `/wp-includes`, `/wp-admin`,
  `/wp-json`, `/wp-login.php`, `/xmlrpc.php`, feeds, and any file extension
  other than `.html`.
  - **Anonymous reads:** the reader's cookie store accepts no cookies, so a
  cookie set by one read is never sent on the next. A test proves it.
  - **Redirects:** scheme, host and port are each compared explicitly.
  - **URL length:** the PHP side cuts the URL to 2,048 characters after
  escaping, and drops any percent-encoding the cut splits. A test uses an
  umlaut-heavy URL.
  - **Source comments:** added for every constant the review named.
  - **Judgement calls:**
    - The page rule now lives in one place (`page.RULE`), and both system
  prompts say page text is data, never instructions.
    - "catalogue" is gone from `Entries.php` and `Preview.php`.
    - A new `tools.py` holds the per-turn call limit and the page tool name, and
  Jev's page types come from the schema.
    - The bootstrap now calls `Proxy` with named arguments.
    - A shared `PlainTitle::of()` cleans titles for both `PageContext` and
  `Preview`.
    - Spec 13 now says the page text is logged with the turn and follows the
  turn log's own retention.

  **Spec fixes:**
  - **Prices:** price elements, sums of money, and short "sign in for pricing"
  lines are removed from page text. The prompt says prices come only from the
  product's price fact, which follows the account.
  - **Delivery:** the delivery-estimate note and money blocks are removed. A
  delivery, shipping or freight question about a page now goes to the team in
  code, whatever Jev says. Both eval rows (EN/DE) routed to the team even though
  Jev read them as document questions.
  - **Query string:** kept for listings (category, filtered, search), dropped
  for single pages.
  - **Hand-off:** the page read no longer forbids handing off to the team.
  - **Call budget:** calls are kept in a fixed order: the route's own calls
  (including the subject's get-product), then the page read, then draft pairs. A
  documents route leaves one call for the model. Anything dropped is recorded
  in the turn log as `dropped_calls`, and unchecked pairs are named in the hint
  so the answer says they weren't checked.
  - **Latency:** the route's calls now run side by side.
  - **Eval:** the acceptance README now says the eval rows pass `draft_ids`
  directly. New EN/DE rows cover a multi-pair draft, delivery and price.

  **Tests and eval:**
  - Tests: pytest 723 (`test_page.py` 30, `test_page_turn.py` 15), PHPUnit 188
  for linker-tools, vitest 122. The browser spec `genie-page.spec.ts` was
  re-run: 48/48, screenshots retaken.
  - Full eval re-run
  (`docs/acceptance/2026-10-18/234/eval/run-20261018T041113.json`): 38 turns
  across all 8 page types plus the new rows, EN/DE, scored 37/38 automatically
  and 38/38 when I read every answer. The one automatic miss was a German
  multi-pair answer that was right but worded differently from the check; I
  widened the check and re-ran those two rows: 2/2 (`run-20261018T041154.json`).
  - An earlier run (`run-20261018T040605.json`) found a real defect: the guest
  account page's line "Your inquiries, documents and prices in one place" was
  being stripped as a price gate. A price gate now needs both a price word and a
  sign-in word, with a test.

  Turn times barely moved, because Jev and the chat model dominate a turn.
  Running the calls side by side only shortens the route-call stage, and only on
  turns that read the page and make other calls. I measured that stage on its
  own with `eval/page/latency.py`, cold cache.

  | | Before | After |
  |---|---|---|
  | Whole turn, same 32 turns, mean (median) | 6.1 s (4.4) | 6.2 s (4.65) |
  | Route-call stage, 3 product-page turns, mean | 4.11 s (one after another) |
  3.45 s (side by side) |

  The per-page-type table is in the PR comment and in
  `docs/acceptance/2026-10-18/234/README.md` §4.

  to #237 after merge.

⏺ #298, the atomic deploy fix, is finished. Codex opened PR #299, its automated
  checks passed, and both halves of my review are now running on it.

  The review focuses on safety, because this changes how every live deploy
  works:
  - Leftover copies: WordPress scans every folder inside plugins/, so a backup
    or staging copy left there could show up as a duplicate plugin. Must-use
    plugins are loaded straight from their folder, so a stray copy of one of
    those could run twice and crash the site.
  - Two-step switch: the gap between the two renames could make WordPress think
    a plugin is missing and switch it off.
  - Symlink alternative: whether a single symlink swap would be safer than two
    renames.
  - Rollback: whether it restores single files (must-use plugins, theme files)
    correctly.

  Codex is still working on #246, #229 and #297. The Genie sub-agents are still
  on #235, #242 and #243.

✻ Crunched for 1m 7s · done 5:24 AM

› Message from general-purpose
  **Standards review: PR #299 (#298)**

  **Safety checks (these pass)**
  - **Where staged and saved copies go.** The staged and saved copies sit next
  to the live path in the same parent directory, as hidden names:
  `.<slug>.next`, `.<slug>.prev` and `.<slug>.prev-absent` (atomic.py
  `publish`/`rollback`). WordPress skips names that start with a dot in
  `get_plugins()`, in `get_mu_plugins()` and when it searches theme directories.
  `wp_get_mu_plugins()` only loads names that end in `.php`, and
  `.linker-error-recorder.php.prev` and `.next` do not, so nothing is loaded
  twice and no duplicate plugin or theme appears in the admin.
  - **The switch is a real rename.** Both directories have the same parent, so
  `mv` is a `rename(2)` and never falls back to a cross-device copy.
  - **An earlier `.prev`** is removed before the switch. That is intended: only
  one previous version is kept.
  - **Second rename fails.** Recovery is automatic. The EXIT trap, plus
  HUP/INT/TERM mapped to `exit 1`, moves `.prev` back, and there are tests for
  it. A SIGKILL or a host crash in that tiny window is not recovered (judgement
  call; the README does not mention it).
  - **Quoting.** Every path goes through `shlex.quote(target.path_of(...))`, and
  `path_of` rejects absolute paths and `..`. The remote shell is `bash -c`, so
  `time` and `TIMEFORMAT` are safe to use.

  **Hard violations**
  1. **AGENTS.md §5, "每次部署先备份、后读回" (back up before every deploy, read
  back after): `steps.rollback_deploy` writes to production with no readback.**
  It returns `[f"restored {path}" ...]` without checking anything. Fix: read
  back what changed (live inode equals the old `.prev`, or `wp plugin list`) and
  do not rely on the README's manual `just cutover verify`.
  2. **AGENTS.md §5, "本轮发现本轮修" (fix what you find in this round): the
  risk of mixing versions is written down but not fixed.** The README warns
  "不把旧部署遗留的备份混当同一批版本" (do not treat backups left by an older
  deploy as part of the same set). The preflight only checks that `.prev`
  exists. If a deploy fails partway, the plugins it did not reach still have
  `.prev` from an older deploy, so rollback silently restores a mix of versions.
  A deploy-id marker checked by the preflight would enforce it.

  **Judgement calls**
  3. **Source may be readable over HTTP (I could not verify this).**
  `.functions.php.prev`, `.*.php.next` and `.*.prev` do not end in `.php`, so
  the web server may serve them as plain text. On the first deploy,
  `.functions.php.prev` is the old site's functions.php. A read-only GET of the
  dotfiles I tried got a 403 for `.htaccess` and 404s otherwise, which proves
  nothing either way. Consider deleting the file `.prev` after verify, or adding
  a deny rule.
  4. **Duplicated Code / Primitive Obsession.** The sibling-name derivation
  appears in both functions:
  `path.with_name(f".{path.name}.next"/.prev/.prev-absent)` in `publish` and
  again in `rollback`. The `restore`/`restore_rollback` shell blocks are
  near-copies, and the `mv`-shim test fixture is repeated three times. One
  `_siblings(target, relative)` helper would fix the first.
  5. **Shotgun Surgery.** `rollback_deploy` rebuilds the deploy inventory with
  its own `glob("*.php") + glob("*.pem")` and `rglob`, separate from
  `deploy_mu_plugins` and `deploy_theme_files`. A change to what deploy selects
  will not reach rollback.
  6. **Mysterious Name.** `timing = atomic.publish(...)` holds the shell
  receipt, not a duration. `publish` returning raw stdout as `str` is itself
  Primitive Obsession.
  7. **Hard-coded values in the README probe.** It hard-codes `root =
  '/home/u388076051/...'` and the slug tuple twice (once in Python, once in the
  shell loop) instead of taking them from config (`plan.NEW_PLUGINS` and the
  target).

  Number-source comments are present (`exit 23`, `0o755`, `timeout=10`, `0.1`).

⏺ The Standards axis on #299 is back. The main safety question is settled:
  staged and backup copies use hidden names (starting with a dot), which
  WordPress skips, so no duplicate plugins appear and nothing loads twice.

  It still found two hard problems:
  - Rollback writes to production without reading back what changed.
  - Rollback can restore a mix of versions after a failed deploy.

  It also flagged one risk worth fixing: backup copies of single files, like the
  old functions.php, sit inside the web root under names that don't end in
  .php. The web server might serve them as plain text, which would expose source
  code.

  I'll combine this with the Spec axis when it arrives.

✻ Sautéed for 11s · done 5:26 AM

⏺ Agent "Standards review of PR 299" finished · 1m 45s

› Message from general-purpose
  **Spec review, PR #299 (ticket #298).** Read-only; nothing was written. The
  live layout check is done: plugins and mu-plugins sit on one filesystem
  (`/dev/sda4`), and no `.next` or `.prev` exist yet.

  **(a) Missing or partial**

  1. "Measure and prove which works on Hostinger" was not done. The README says
  it took the two renames and did "没有引入 `current` 符号链接", with no symlink
  trial and no measurement on Hostinger. This matters because of how `__DIR__`
  works:
     - With a symlink swap, `__DIR__` and the Composer autoloader resolve to the
  real release path. A request that is already running keeps loading the old
  release, and opcache keys change with the release.
     - With a rename at the same path, a request that started before the switch
  can autoload a class from the new tree after the switch. Opcache entries with
  timestamp revalidation can also serve old bytecode next to new files.
     - So "a request loading a mix of old and new plugin files" is made rare but
  not removed. The gap between the two renames also lets a request run with the
  plugin missing: the front end skips it silently, and
  `validate_active_plugins()` on `plugins.php` deactivates it.
  2. "A deploy on live leaves zero new entries in `fatal-errors-*.log`… hitting
  a REST endpoint in a loop" is not done yet. The README marks it
  "未完成（Claude）". It cannot count as done until that run is attached.
  3. Cross-plugin consistency is not handled. `deploy()` builds, uploads and
  switches one plugin at a time, and mu-plugins and theme files go out before
  all of them. The window runs for the build and upload time of each later
  plugin, which is seconds to minutes. Real cross-plugin dependencies exist:
     - `linker-commerce/includes/Account/AdminAccessNotice.php` uses
  `Linker\I18n\Languages`.
     - `linker-i18n/includes/Translation/DigestBlock.php` uses
  `Linker\Commerce\Digest\Block`.

     This is the same type of fatal the ticket names, only between plugins. The
  fix is to stage all three plugins first, then switch them in one shell. The
  doc admits it: "这不构成全站单事务".

  **(b) Scope creep:** none material. `rollback-deploy` is a command the ticket
  asked for.

  **(c) Implemented but wrong or risky**

  4. Duplicate loading is avoided:
     - Hidden `.<slug>.next/.prev` are skipped by `get_plugins()`, which ignores
  names starting with a dot.
     - mu backups end in `.next/.prev`, not `.php`, so `wp_get_mu_plugins` does
  not load them.

     There is a side effect: `.functions.php.prev`,
  `.linker-site-guards.php.prev` and `.linker-engine-pin.php.prev` sit under
  `public_html` and may be served as plain text, which exposes PHP source. The
  repo is public, so this is low severity, but it is not stated anywhere.
  5. Rollback is right for single files. Deploy does `cp -p live→.prev` then `mv
  -f .next live`, and rollback does `mv -f .prev live`. Both are single
  renames, so the file is never missing. `.prev-absent` handles files that were
  new on that deploy. Directory rollback has the same gap between two renames as
  in point 1.
  6. The probe is safe and can be re-run:
     - It writes nothing except the deploy itself and local JSON.
     - Its SSH calls are `find`/`stat` only.
     - It checks the inode of the old live directory against `.prev`, which
  proves the rename happened.

     It is weak in two ways:
     - It hits one endpoint (`catalog-search`), which may not load the Contact
  or Email code that failed on 2026-10-17.
     - It only shows that one deploy happened to produce no fatals. That proves
  nothing about the in-flight and cross-plugin windows above.

  **Verdict:** done-when #1 (a test shows a rename) and #3 (one-command rollback
  documented) are met. #2 is open. The ticket's "measure and prove" step is
  skipped, and cross-plugin atomicity is a real gap against the goal of zero
  fatals.

⏺ Both axes for #299 are in. The idea is sound, but there are three real gaps:
  1. Plugins switch one at a time, minutes apart. Our plugins call each other's
     code, so a request in between can still crash the way it did on 2026-10-17.
  2. Rollback can mix versions after a failed deploy, and nothing reads back 
     after it.
  3. Backup copies sit inside the public web root, where the web server might
     serve them as plain text.

  All decided. Posting the review and resuming the #298 thread:

⏺ #298 (atomic deploy, PR #299) needs another round; Codex is working on it now.
  Its basic approach holds: backup copies use hidden names that WordPress
  ignores, and a failed switch restores itself. The review found three gaps, and
  I decided all of them:

  - Switch everything together. Today each plugin is switched separately,
    seconds to minutes apart. Our plugins call each other's code, so a request
    in that gap could still crash the way it did on 2026-10-17. The new version
    uploads everything first, then swaps it all in one step.
  - Rollback restores one consistent version. Each deploy records an id, and
    rollback refuses if its backups come from different deploys. It also checks
    the live site afterwards, as every production write requires.
  - Nothing left in the public folder. After a deploy passes its checks, the
    backup copies move out of the public web folder, so the web server can never
    serve old source files.

  I chose plain file renames over a symlink swap, because WordPress plugin paths
  break easily with symlinks. The reasoning goes into the deploy spec.

  The watcher was already running and now tracks this job. Other work still
  running:
  - Codex: #246, #229 and #297.
  - Genie sub-agents: #235, #242 and #243.
