✻ Cooked for 57s · done 5:31 AM

› Message from general-purpose
  **Standards review of PR #300 (origin/main...pr300, 31 files)**

  **Hard violations:** none found.
  - **i18n:** I decompiled both `.mo` files and compared them. 791 → 795
  messages. No msgstr was lost or changed. The 4 new strings all have German
  translations. The untranslated count is 49 on both main and the PR, so nothing
  new is untranslated.
  - **Numbers:** 465, 1–65535 and 8/80 all carry source comments (IANA,
  PHPMailer, `mail-root-cause.md`).
  - **Secrets:** no eval or shell; `parse_ini_file(..., INI_SCANNER_RAW)` is
  used and its errors are silenced. `SMTPDebug=0`. Errors from internal mail are
  reduced to a fixed string, and tests assert that no fake password reaches the
  log, job meta or options. No new plugin or dependency.

  **Security checks (all pass):**
  - **Shared mailer reuse:** WordPress already calls `isMail()` before
  `phpmailer_init` (pluggable.php:505, outside its send try/catch). On top of
  that, `configure()` resets the mailer first, and `Outbox::attempt` resets it
  again in `finally`. Customer mail cannot end up on SMTP: `$internal` is false,
  so the configuration is null.
  - **TLS:** `SMTPOptions` is untouched, so certificate checking keeps
  PHPMailer's default.
  - **From vs login:** a From that differs from the SMTP user throws, and the
  exception goes into the retry path.
  - **Test mode:** `redirect()` runs on the delivery copy and the marker
  survives it.
  - `clearCustomHeader` exists in the bundled PHPMailer 7.1.1.

  **Judgement calls and smells:**
  1. **Test-only branch in production code** (`InternalSmtp::configure`):
  `'127.0.0.1' === $config['host'] ? '' : ...` and `SMTPAutoTLS = '127.0.0.1'
  !== ...`. This lets AUTH run unencrypted to a host named only by a string.
  `reset()` also does not put `SMTPAutoTLS` or `Port` back to their defaults.
  Neither leaks into customer mail.
  2. **Hidden dependency (Feature Envy):** `configuration()` creates its own `(
  new Options() )->notify_sender()`, although the class takes its other input
  (the path) through the constructor. That is inconsistent with the injection
  style in `Outbox`.
  3. **Primitive Obsession / duplicated literal:** the marker is stored as a raw
  `'X-Linker-Internal: yes'` line, yet `InternalSmtp::configure` writes the
  bare name `'X-Linker-Internal'` again for `clearCustomHeader`.
  4. **Duplicated Code** (`Outbox`): `'Internal notification delivery failed.'`
  appears twice, once in `remember_error` and once in the new `catch`.
  5. **Shotgun Surgery:** each producer has to remember the marker.
  `TeamEmail::get_headers`, `TeamMessageEmail::get_headers` (a parallel copy,
  because it extends `ContactEmail`) and `Monitor` all add
  `Message::INTERNAL_HEADER`, and the spec now writes "must carry" as the
  contract for any future producer.
  6. **Misplaced class:** `Health\InternalMail` implements `Digest\Block` and is
  wired into `Digest\Blocks`, while the other three built-in blocks live in
  `Digest\`. The `Blocks.php` docblock now says "the four this plugin builds",
  and one of the four sits in another module.
  7. **Config parse edge case:** in RAW mode an unquoted `;` silently cuts the
  value short. I checked: `A=ab;cd` reads as `"ab"`. A password containing `;`
  would still be reported as "configured" by the health block and then fail to
  authenticate. The spec does say to use double quotes for special characters,
  so this is documented.
  8. **Python fixture standard:** `tests/fixtures/smtp_sink.py` has no type

  catches the fully qualified `\PHPMailer\PHPMailer\Exception`. The `use` order

⏺ Agent "Standards review of PR 300" finished · 1m 54s

› Message from general-purpose
  **Spec review of PR #300 (#297)**

  **Coverage is correct.** Six producers carry `X-Linker-Internal`: NewCustomer,
  VatReview, NewInquiry and Digest (all through `TeamEmail::get_headers`), the
  contact `TeamMessageEmail`, and the health alert in `Monitor`. Customer mail
  does not carry the marker: CustomerOrderEmail, ReceiptEmail and
  CustomerApprovalEmail are unmarked, as are Woo and core mail. On main, #246
  (guest inquiry) has no separate sender; it goes through NewInquiryEmail.
  Nothing internal is missed and no customer mail is wrongly included.

  **(a) Missing or partial**
  1. Spec: "the failure is visible, never silent." The health block checks only
  that the config file exists, parses, and its user matches the internal sender
  (`authenticated()` = `configuration() !== null`). Consider a wrong password, a
  revoked mailbox or a blocked port. The block reports "configured". Every
  internal mail then retries on SMTP and is marked failed after the last retry.
  The digest and health alert that should report this also go over the same
  broken SMTP. The only trace is the `linker-mail` WooCommerce log. That is a
  real path to silently losing internal notifications. There is no `mail()`
  fallback when SMTP fails at send time, and no failure signal that avoids SMTP.
  2. Spec: "credentials from a server env file outside web root." The path is
  built from `getenv('HOME')`. Registration, inquiry and contact mails are sent
  during the web request. Nobody has checked whether Hostinger's web PHP
  (LiteSpeed) sets HOME. If it does not, web-triggered mail quietly falls back
  to `mail()`. The digest still says "configured", because it runs from CLI cron
  where HOME is set. Every readback in README step 3 runs through `wp eval`,
  and the single acceptance digest comes from CLI cron. Neither proves the web
  context uses SMTP.
  3. Throttle (judge item 3). Spec 11 sources only the `mail()` limits (8/80
  from `mail-root-cause.md` §3/§9). It does not cite or compare the per-mailbox
  SMTP limit that the same research records (line 63: free-trial mailbox
  100/day, Business Starter 1000/day, rolling 24h). Two facts are also
  unrecorded: the sunriver mailbox's plan, and that its quota is shared with
  sunriver's own sending. Sharing the 8/80 throttle is conservative and so
  respects both limits, but the spec has no source for the SMTP side.

  **(b) Scope creep**
  - None of substance. The CI step, the spec 74 block and the copy review all
  follow from the ticket.

  **(c) Implemented but wrong or risky**
  - An internal message queued before deploy whose From differs from the auth
  user throws. Each retry fails and the message ends up marked failed. The spec
  says "Outbox retries use the same path". In practice such a message can never
  be delivered, when it could have gone through `mail()`. This is minor.

  **README procedure (judge item 4)**
  - It matches AGENTS §5: backup (`just cutover backup`), readback of
  boolean-only config and routing, one real digest to sunriver plus the
  already-allowed Bcc, a ticket record and rollback steps.
  - The owner places the credential and Claude never reads it.
  - Gap: the readback should add one check from web-request context (see a2)
  before treating the digest pass as proof for all six types.

  Files: `plugins/linker-commerce/includes/Mail/InternalSmtp.php`,
  `plugins/linker-commerce/includes/Health/InternalMail.php`,
  `plugins/linker-commerce/includes/Mail/Outbox.php`,
  `docs/specs/11-commerce.md`, `docs/acceptance/2026-10-18/297/README.md`.

  them into one; the diff against main is unchanged in meaning.

  ### Standards, hard items
  1. **German JS strings.** All 66 messages of `build/genie.js` are now in
  `linker-tools-de_DE_formal.po`, with references to that bundle.
     - `wp i18n make-json --no-purge` generates
  `linker-tools-de_DE_formal-c2be3a742ec1f5e619cd2beb8d234138.json`. I removed
  the hand-written handle-named JSON.
     - `docs/acceptance/2026-10-18/235/i18n-proof.txt` shows, against
  origin/main, 0 messages missing and 0 translations changed; #235 adds 18.
     - The generated file is in `.prettierignore`, as linker-commerce already
  does for its JSON.
  2. **Guest self-email.**
     - **Confirm first:** a guest first gets a fixed-copy email with a one-time
  link (24 h, only its hash stored) and no conversation text. The conversation
  is sent only after the link is opened.
     - **Bot guard:** a honeypot plus a signed start time (at least 3 s, at most
  a day).
     - **Site-wide cap:** 10 Genie emails a day (a tenth of the host's 100),
  plus 3 per connection an hour. Over either limit the request is refused, not
  queued.
     - **Atomic limit:** the new `RateLimit` checks and increments in one
  database statement.
     - **Tests:** guest without confirmation sends no transcript; trap or timing
  failure sends nothing; at the cap `wp_mail()` is never called. Eight parallel
  processes against a limit of 3 get exactly 3 through.
  3. **Key rotation.** A new browser key is issued after the sign-in claim and
  on logout (logout also forgets the current conversation). User Switching does
  not claim. Each has a test.

  **One deviation you should know about:** the start time is signed with an
  HMAC, not a WordPress nonce as on the inquiry door. On g235 every guest
  request failed the nonce check, because the nonce follows WooCommerce's
  session cookie, which appears between page render and send.

  ### Judgement fixes and Spec 1–3
  - **Judgement fixes:**
    - `title` is gone from the payload.
    - Cookie writes share one helper.
    - There is one role function.
    - There is one limiter class.
    - The outcome words have one definition per language, and the Proxy and
  `turn.ts` both read them.
    - The `|| 90` fallback is gone; the days come only from the page.
    - The new `genie.css` rules use tokens with fallbacks, and the file header
  is corrected.
  - **Spec 1:** the email lists each answer's products (linked name, model, in
  the email's language), earlier turns included.
  - **Spec 2:** a test asserts From and Reply-To `Linker <sales@de-linker.com>`,
  with no Bcc.
  - **Spec 3:** the list orders by last activity, which a new turn updates and
  naming or sign-in never touch. Continuing an old conversation moves it to the
  top, and sign-in does not reorder.

⏺ #235 (Genie keeps every conversation) is merged after two review rounds. It
  goes out with the next deploy.

  Guests keep all their conversations in their own browser, and on sign-in they
  move into the account. Anyone can delete a single conversation. Emailing a
  conversation to yourself is protected against abuse:
  - a guest first confirms their address through a one-time link;
  - there's a bot check;
  - the site sends at most 10 of these emails a day and refuses beyond that, so
    the shared mail quota stays free for inquiries.

  The email lists the products from the answers. The conversation list puts the
  most recently used conversation first.

  A new problem found while reviewing it, #302: the German text of the Solution
  Configurator (201 of its 209 strings) was typed straight into a generated file
  instead of the source translation file. The next normal translation rebuild
  would silently turn the German configurator back to English. Codex is fixing
  it and adding a check that blocks this from happening again.

  One thing to verify on #246 (inquiries open to everyone): the #235 work showed
  that a guest's security token on a form can stop working when WooCommerce
  starts its session between page load and submit. #246's inquiry and contact
  forms use the same kind of token, so in its next review I'll require a test of
  a real guest submitting from a fresh browser.

  - Codex: #246, #229, #297, #298, #302

  - #236 starts after #242 merges.

  ## #242 Genie · prices follow the account: PR open, CI green, waiting for your
  review

  **PR:** https://github.com/AssassinFlash/de-linker/pull/301 (branch
  `feat/242-account-prices`, rebased on origin/main c12a9a22, 5 commits). Both
  CI jobs passed (`check` and `mu-smoke`). I commented "PR #301 待评审" on #242
  and stopped there: nothing merged or deployed, no production writes.

  **What it does:**
  - **Same price rule as the site.** Genie uses `CommerceState` and nothing
  else. The shell's existing short-lived token (`UserContext`, valid 120 s) used
  to go only to `inquiry-status`. It now also goes to `search-products` and
  `get-product`, so they answer for the customer. It is never sent on
  compare-products, check-compatibility, size-hint or handoff-team. The scope
  and lifetime are in `docs/specs/13-genie.md`, new section 「价格跟账号走」.
  - **`partner` comes from the price rule.** The proxy fills it from the filter
  `linker_commerce_visitor_sees_prices`, which linker-commerce answers with
  `CommerceState::visitor_sees_prices()`. It is never "logged in", as the #292
  judge needs.
  - **The ability returns the price line.** The `commerce` object now carries
  `price_text` (the product page's own price line, German format for German
  turns) or `price_reason` (login / pending / restricted / project / unpriced).
  The reason order comes from the new `CommerceState::page_reason()`.
  - **The model never writes a price.** `ground.py` rejects any price
  placeholder in text.
  - **The service fills each card's price.** The new `prices.fill` does this on
  both the normal and fallback paths. For a card no tool priced, it reads
  `get-product` as the customer and logs that call. The model cannot see or set
  the card's price field.
  - **The card shows exactly what the product page shows.** Either the amount,
  or the page's own sentence; the German text is copied word for word from the
  linker-commerce catalog.
  - **Quality check.** The citation check holds a card's price to the same two
  conditions as a price in the text: the tool returned it as visible, and the
  account is approved. Spec 33 is updated.

  **Product-page problems in the same rule, fixed in this branch
  (approved/pending/restricted views only; the guest's page text is
  unchanged):**
  - An approved account on rfq product 1957 saw "Not available for your
  account"; it now sees "Quoted per project".
  - 1957's German twin (6800) had no `rfq_only` flag, so the German product page
  showed approved accounts €33,250. `is_rfq_only()` now reads the English
  product's flag.
  - The compare page's "Net price" row told every account without prices to
  "sign in". It now uses the product page's line (`ProductView::price_html`).

› Message from general-purpose
  **Standards review, PR #301 (#242)**

  The diff was taken against the merge-base c12a9a22. origin/main has since
  moved on with #296/#235.

  **Hard violations**
  1. **linker-tools German translations exist only in the hand-edited JSON.**
  `plugins/linker-tools/languages/linker-tools-de_DE_formal-linker-tools-genie.j
  son` gained 5 strings ("Quoted per project", "Price on request" and three
  more). `linker-tools-de_DE_formal.po` has none of them: grep finds 0, while
  the older strings such as "Privacy Policy" are present. This breaks AGENTS §4
  (`@wordpress/i18n` + `wp i18n make-json`) and the EN/DE-in-sync rule. The next
  make-json run from the .po drops all five German card sentences.
  2. **The PR's commerce .po/.pot/.mo is out of date against main.** Against the
  merge-base, the compiled `linker-commerce-de_DE_formal.mo` matches exactly:
  no msgstr lost or changed, the tracked .mo equals the .po, and the 2 new
  msgids are exempt schema descriptions. Against current origin/main, the PR's
  catalog lacks about 15 msgstr from #235 (the Genie email strings). The branch
  needs a rebase and a fresh `just i18n-commerce` before merge, or those German
  strings are lost.

  **Security (judgement calls)**
  - **Token transport and logging are fine.** The token goes only in
  `USER_CONTEXT_HEADER` on 3 named abilities, and `partner` defaults to false
  (`true === apply_filters(..., false)`). `record()` logs the name, arguments
  and result, never the header. `AbilityError` text includes params but not
  headers. `prices.py` logs `error` only.
  - **The token is not tied to one ability (unchanged code, scope widened by
  this PR).** `UserContext::is_ability_request` uses `str_contains(REQUEST_URI,
  '/wp-abilities/')`. A query string containing that text is accepted, and the
  token works on any ability for 120 s. This PR now sends it on three abilities,
  not one.
  - **The 120 s lifetime has no source comment.** `UserContext::TTL = 120` has
  none (§4 "每个数字与阈值必须有来源注释"). The PR repeats "120 seconds" in the
  `abilities.py` docstring and the `schema.py` description, still without a
  source.
  - **The post-run get-product calls are outside the #234 tool budget
  (`MAX_TOOL_CALLS`).** In `prices.fill`/`commerce_lookup` they are capped only
  by the cards limit (`max_length=4`). Each call can take 3 attempts × 20 s
  timeout, which can overrun the shell's 60 s turn.

  **Smells**
  - **Duplicated Code / Repeated Switches**
    - The reason→sentence mapping exists three times: `ProductView::price_html`,
  `GenieCard.priceLine` (a second text domain), and `ProductCommerce::reason`.
    - That last one maps `CommerceState::LOGIN`→`'login'`, which is the same
  value, through a nested ternary.
    - `prices.REASONS` repeats `schema.PriceReason` in the same package
  (`get_args(PriceReason)` would do).
    - The `has_filter('linker_commerce_facts_product_id')` guard is copied from
  `SelectionFacts`.
  - **Speculative Generality / test seam in production code:**
  `\function_exists( 'has_filter' )` in `CommerceState::is_rfq_only`.
  - **Primitive Obsession:** `ProductCommerce::of(..., string $lang = 'en')`
  with `'de' === $lang`.
  - **Middle Man / Refused Bequest:** `PriceDisplay::sees_prices( $sees ) {
  unset( $sees ); ... }` overrides any other filter callback.
  - **§5 "PHP 不解决设计问题" (minor, a judgement call):**
  `ProductView::price_html` is now public and returns the worded sentences that
  `Comparison` prints in the compare price cell. That is text, not markup or
  style, so it is not a hard breach.
  - **Docstring numbering:** `ground.py` inserts the "price rule" paragraph
  between items 7 and 8 of its numbered list.

  No banned model routes or legacy references were added. `tv_b2b_product_mode`
  is an existing data key that the diff does not touch.

  None of substance. The three fixes are bugs in the same price rule
  (本轮发现本轮修), and the kickoff completion line ("与产品页一致") needs them.

  1. **The site now has two rule orderings.** "站内唯一的价格规则是
  CommerceState…不另写一套". The new `CommerceState::page_reason()` checks the
  account first, while the existing `reason()` (used by PriceDisplay,
  StoreApiPrices and DraftSummary) checks the project first. A guest on 1957
  gets "Quoted per project" on shop cards and the draft, but the Genie card and
  product page read login. The PR should either justify the split or unify it.

  `linker-commerce-de_DE_formal.po` (PriceDisplay, "Angebot pro Projekt"), so

  accept it.** This goes through `linker_commerce_facts_product_id`, the same
  path SelectionFacts, DocumentLibrary and Materials already use. Polylang free
  can't sync Woo meta natively, and the English product is the one hand-edited
  source. The leftover risk: the stale `tv_b2b_product_mode` value on 6800 stays
  in the data, and any future direct `get_post_meta` read would bypass the
  swap. Note this in spec 11.
  4. **The judge remit still says "the answer may state a product price" when
  the account is approved** (`rubric.py:48`). The new spec forbids prices in
  text ("模型正文不写价格数字"). `ground.py` enforces that, so the impact is
  low, but the remit and the spec disagree.

✻ Crunched for 16s · done 6:03 AM

› Message from general-purpose
  **PR #299 round 2 (head 3fd82d13): no blocking defects. Tests: 107 passed in
  14 s.**

  I rendered the real publish, archive and rollback scripts using the 3 plugin
  directories, an existing mu-plugin, a new mu-plugin and a theme functions.php.
  I also checked bash errexit inside `{ time {…}; } 2>&1`: the failing mv fires
  the EXIT trap.

  1. **Switch** (`atomic.py:259-297`): all 9 renames run in one `bash -c` with
  `set -eu`. One `restore_all` EXIT trap undoes them in reverse, and
  HUP/INT/TERM go through `exit 1` to that trap. Backups are checked absent
  before the switch, so `.prev` existing means "already switched". The manifest
  and `latest` pointer are written inside the trap's coverage, and the trap is
  cleared last. A plugin directory is never left missing on a catchable failure.
  SIGKILL can't be caught: it would leave `.prev` in place. Deploy then refuses
  because a sibling exists, and rollback refuses because live drifted, so
  recovery is manual.
  2. **Archive** (`atomic.py:337-390`): `_same_filesystem` runs before the
  switch (line 198). It also uses `pwd -P` to reject a backup root inside the
  web root. Moves are renames into `~/deploy-prev/<id>/files/` after `test !
  -e`. Pruning of older batches runs only after the archived manifest commits.
  Nothing in `public_html` is deleted except `.next`/`.prev-absent` siblings and
  paths that were absent before.
  3. **Rollback**: preflight names mixed deploy ids, inventory mismatches,
  backup inode mismatches and live drift before any write. Restore is rename;
  files use a hard-linked `.next`. Readback compares device:inode.
  `steps.py:269-287` then runs `wp plugin list` (must be active) and verify, and
  returns non-zero on failure.
  4. **Quoting**: every path goes through `shlex.quote` or `_quoted`. The deploy
  id is regex-checked, and manifest paths go through `path_of` and must match
  the repo inventory.
  5. **Probe** (`plan.py` `DEPLOY_PROBE_ENDPOINTS`): `/`, `/contact/`,
  `/proforma-request/`, a product page, `linker/v1/catalog-search`,
  `wp/v2/pages?lang=de`, `linker-ai/v1/genie/conversation`. All are GETs. The
  Genie GET never creates a conversation (`Proxy.php:300-307`), and fatal logs
  are read by stat only. It is read-only apart from the deploy.

  **Non-blocking:**
  - `atomic.py:273`: recovery runs `rm -rf live; mv previous live`. The live
  directory is gone for the whole rm, and if rm fails partway, `.prev` gets
  moved *inside* `live`. Better: `mv live .x.failed && mv previous live && rm
  -rf .x.failed`.
  - `atomic.py:289-298`: `2>&1` sends mv's stderr into the output that `float()`
  parses. Any extra output after a good switch raises `ValueError` rather than
  `CutoverError`. Activation and language packs would be skipped, leaving the
  deploy pending.
  - No command re-runs verify and archive on a pending deploy (lost ssh reply,
  transient verify failure); only `rollback-deploy` unblocks. Until then,
  `.functions.php.prev` and the mu `.prev` files stay in `public_html`.
  - The archive prune only matches folders containing `manifest.json`, so a
  batch folder from a killed publish (it has none) is never removed.
  - The probe runs 7 cache-busted PHP request loops for the whole deploy, local
  Composer build included. That may hit shared-hosting process limits; consider
  sampling only around the switch.
  - `rollback-deploy` on a batch where a plugin didn't exist before always
  reports "missing", which is expected.

  The temporary worktree has been removed.

  - **Wording:** "Kontaktanfrage" instead of "Anfrage" (which already means an

✻ Sautéed for 1m 32s · done 6:20 AM

› Message from general-purpose
  Standards review of PR #303 (diff `origin/main...pr303`, docs/acceptance left
  out)

  **Hard violations**
  1. **The PR is behind main and does not merge.** `git merge-tree` reports
  conflicts in the commerce `.po`, `.pot` and `.mo`, the linker-tools `.po` and
  `.mo`, and `build/manifest.json`, because #297 and #302 merged after the
  branch point (781f5a22). As it stands, the PR's `.mo` files compared with
  origin/main lose 9 msgids in linker-commerce (the #297 SMTP status strings)
  and 210 in linker-tools (the #302 configurator strings). Compared with the
  merge base, nothing is lost or changed. Before merging it needs a rebase, then
  `just i18n-commerce`, `just i18n-tools` and the coverage gate run again. This
  falls under the i18n rule in AGENTS.md §4.
  2. **Wrong class named in `ContactRequest.php`.** The class docblock and the
  docblock on `do_action` both name `Contact\Emails\GenieContactEmail`, which
  does not exist. The real class is `Linker\Commerce\Genie\ContactEmail`
  (Mysterious Name: the reference is stale).

  **Judgement calls and smells**
  - **Duplicated Code in `ContactGuard.php`.** It re-implements what
  `ConversationMail` already does: the HMAC start stamp (`hash_hmac(...
  wp_salt('nonce'))`), `MIN_SECONDS = 3`, `LIMIT = 3`, the `REMOTE_ADDR`
  `address()` helper and the hourly-plus-daily `RateLimit` pair. It also drops
  the sibling's `STAMP_LIFETIME`, so a signed `started` never expires. That is
  low risk, since GET hands out fresh stamps to anyone, but the two guards now
  behave differently.
  - **Duplicated labels across the two plugins (Shotgun Surgery).**
  `ContactEmail::reason_label()` and `ContactRequest::reason_label()` hold the
  same four strings in two text domains. The constants `ACTION` and
  `MAILBOX_FILTER` are also defined in both plugins. This follows the existing
  SEND_FILTER pattern, but changing a reason now means editing Python, TS and
  two PHP files.
  - **Dependency in the wrong direction in `log.py`.** `from linker_genie import
  contact`: the storage layer now parses domain output through
  `contact.offered()` (Feature Envy).
  - **Placeholder model name in the turn log (`app.py`).** `model="contact" if
  code_made else model_name()` writes a name that isn't a model into the field
  the model-routing audits read. A null or `"none"` would be clearer.
  - **phpstan.** `phpstan.neon.dist` in linker-commerce now scans
  `../linker-tools/includes/Genie`, which couples one plugin's static analysis
  to the other plugin's source.
  - **Bootstrap duplicate.** `linker-tools.php` still has `new QualityPage(
  $quality, new Thread() )` alongside the shared `$thread`. This was there
  before the PR, but the PR edits the "used N times" comments right next to it.

  **Checked and in order**
  - `ContactEmail` extends `TeamEmail`, so it inherits `Message::mark_internal`,
  `notify_to()` and the Bcc list.
  - Reply-To is safe: the guest address goes through `sanitize_email` and
  `is_email` and the guest name is always empty. A signed-in customer's name
  comes from billing meta or `display_name`, the same as the existing
  `TeamEmail` pattern.
  - The template escapes everything (`esc_html`, `nl2br(esc_html)`, `esc_url`).
  - The admin link goes to `QualityPage::CAPABILITY = manage_woocommerce`.
  - The guest page URL is limited to this site's own host (`own_url`).
  - The honeypot is checked first, then the signed start, then the address
  check. A request already sent for the same conversation (message count
  unchanged) is not sent again. `RateLimit` takes a place per connection and one
  of the site's 20 a day, so the phrase table can only show the card; it cannot
  push extra mail.
  - Every number cites its source: `DAILY` (Throttle 80 / ConversationMail 10),
  `SAME = 0.7` (eval `repeats.json`), `REPEATS`, `SUMMARY_MESSAGES`,
  `MIN_SECONDS`.
  - `genie.css` adds no new raw values: every hex and token already exists on
  main.
  - No old-system identifiers. No new model channels. The service logs only the
  matched phrase, never the email address.
  - The tests put back the rate-limit counters and `REMOTE_ADDR` after each run,
  and use `@example.test` addresses.

⏺ The #303 Standards check found two blocking problems:
  - Behind main: the branch would lose translations if merged now. It is missing
    9 strings from #297 and 210 configurator strings from #302, so it needs a
    rebase.
  - Wrong class name: a code comment refers to a class that doesn't exist.

  It also found code duplicated from #235's "email this conversation"
  protection, and that copy has one difference: its signed start time never
  expires. I'll put these together with the Spec findings when that check
  arrives.

› Message from general-purpose
  **Spec review of PR #303 (#243): 3 changes needed before merge (findings 1, 2
  and 6); the rest (3, 4, 5, 7) are lighter fixes or notes**

  **(a) Missing or partial**

  1. **Plan for joining #290 is wrong (Q3).** The spec says: "合并后…由
  `ContactRequest` 调 #246 的发送入口…本票的 meta 与团队邮件内容不变". In
  `pr290:plugins/linker-commerce/includes/Inquiry/Sender.php`,
  `Sender::message()` does three things that break that sentence:
     - It creates a Woo order set to `RECEIVED`, which sends the customer
  receipt. That contradicts "不给客户发邮件".
     - It sends #246's team mail instead of `ContactEmail`, so the mail content
  does change.
     - It needs a `Submission` with a name, and the guest card collects only an
  email.

     #246 and #290 do not mention #243 or Genie. Keeping the record as meta for
  now is acceptable, but the integration has to be written on #246/#290 or into
  whichever PR merges second. The spec line must be corrected. AGENTS.md says
  "本轮发现的问题本轮修".
  2. **The confirmation does not last (Q2).** The one-working-day promise is on
  the card (B3, B4, B9), and the team mail says it too. But blocks render only
  for the latest turn (`App.tsx` `turn?.blocks`). In `en-1440-guest-after.png`
  the sent card is gone after the next question. The spec says
  "卡就是确认…重载后仍是这一态". Guests get no receipt and lose the only proof.
  Contact-form senders do get a `ReceiptEmail`, so the two doors behave
  differently.
  3. **Only one "unhappy" sentence per language was evaluated** (cases.json). It
  meets the completion criterion, but just barely.

  **(b) Scope creep**

  None of substance. Changes to QualityPage, the transcript and
  `TeamEmail::notify(null)` are needed by the handoff.

  **(c) Implemented but wrong or inconsistent**

  4. **The service card conflicts with the acceptance text (Q1).** Goal 1 allows
  the card: "照旧一句话转团队，同时可一键'让 Linker 联系我'". But
  "范围外意图只有 handoff 块" and R8 "范围外只有邮箱句" were left unchanged. The
  card is appended after `ground`, so the check never sees it. Update the
  guarantee and R8. The card is also noisy: `decide()` returns `service` before
  checking `offers[0]`, so back-to-back logistics questions get a card every
  turn. The spec table marks this as intended ("每次都提供"), but it goes
  against the spirit of "不连续提议两次".
  5. **The repeat detection is weak (Q4).** The 31 pairs are synthetic, written
  by the implementer, and the safe cut range is narrow (0.67–0.75). My probes
  against `same_question`:
     - It joins genuine refinements: "Which battery fits SAJ H2-10K-T3?" against
  "…with backup?" or "…for 15 kWh?" counts as the same question.
     - It joins sibling models: "Datasheet SAJ H2-10K-T3-EU" against
  "H2-8K-T3-EU" counts as the same question.

     Three narrowing follow-ups would therefore get an offer. The risk is low
  because the card only adds and never mutes, but the spec should not call the
  threshold "测定" until real follow-up pairs are added as negatives.
  6. **The phrase table cuts off product answers.** "Can the inverter talk to
  the team portal?" matches `talk to the team` and skips Jev and the model
  entirely. `team`, `expert` and `engineer` in `_PERSON` are too broad.
  7. **Genie is not muted (Q5): this is met.** The next turn routes normally
  (`*-after.png`, `App.test.tsx`), and the dissatisfied and repeat offers keep
  the normal answer.

  - Copy review: `docs/acceptance/2026-10-18/243/copy-review.md` (29 items).
  - Trigger eval: 20/20.

  `plugins/linker-commerce/tests/php/Integration/GenieContactTest.php` cover

  `rest_pre_dispatch`, and only for a GET whose parsed route is
  `inquiry-status`, `search-products` or `get-product`. It signs out on
  `rest_post_dispatch` and marks that answer `no-store, private`.
     - Unit tests cover the three abilities, other abilities, a forged query
  string, POST, an existing session winning, and sign-out.
     - The instance test proves it over HTTP:
  `users/me?x=/wp-abilities/…/get-product/run` with a token returned 200 on main
  and returns 401 on this branch.
  4. **TTL source.** `UserContext::TTL` now says why it is 120: twice the
  shell's 60 s turn timeout. Docstrings and the schema description refer to it
  by name instead of repeating the number.
  5. **Post-run `get-product` lookups.**
     - They run side by side until 50 s after the turn starts
  (`LOOKUP_DEADLINE`, sourced against the shell's 60 s).
     - Lookups that answer are logged as tool calls; misses are reported in
  `price_failures`.
     - A test shows a slow ability cannot push the turn past the deadline.
     - A card left without price data shows the account's sentence for a guest,
  pending or restricted account. For an approved account it shows no line,
  because that line depends on the product.

  **Judgement calls (all done)**
  - **One sentence mapping.** It lives on the server: `price_line` is
  `ProductView::price_html` rendered in the turn's locale. `GenieCard` only
  displays it.
  - **Smaller items.**
    - `prices.REASONS` derives from `schema.PriceReason`.
    - `ProductCommerce::reason` uses a `match`.
    - The `has_filter` seam is gone from `is_rfq_only`.
    - The language comes from `ProductText::lang` and the new
  `ProductText::locale`.
    - `PriceDisplay::sees_prices()` takes no argument and is documented as the
  only answer to its filter.
    - `ground.py`'s checks are numbered 1–9.

  **Spec**
  1. **One function, two surfaces.** `CommerceState::reason( $product, CARD|PAGE
  )`. Each order cites what the live site did on 2026-10-18:
     - CARD (Woo's own price HTML, Store API, solution draft and inquiry lines)
  names the product first: the live Store API answer for 1957 to a guest is
  "Quoted per project".
     - PAGE (product page, compare page, Genie card) names the account first:

✻ Sautéed for 19s · done 9:43 AM

❯ What about you then? I'd like to go over how the works done.                  

⏺ Right now my side is mostly waiting:
  - Production backup: started at 01:26 and takes about 45 minutes. It's the
    first step of tonight's one-step deploy.
  - Genie tickets: #243 (hand-off to the team, waiting on #246) and #236 (photos
    and attachments) are with my sub-agents.
  - Codex: I'm still watching #246 and #229 until they land.

  Here's how each ticket moves.

  1. Build. Genie tickets go to one of my sub-agents; everything else goes to
  Codex. Each works in its own copy of the repo and its own local site, synced
  from live, writes tests first, and opens a PR. When the automated checks pass,
  it comments "待评审" (ready for review).

  2. Review. Two review agents run in parallel. One checks the repo's rules, the
  other checks against the ticket. I make every call myself and post the
  decisions on the PR; you don't get asked. I also re-check the riskiest claims
  myself rather than trusting the report: translation files byte by byte, pixel
  captures, real mail headers, security points. Usually two rounds, sometimes
  three.

  3. Ship. I merge, back up, deploy, verify on live, and record the result on
  the ticket. Problems found along the way become new tickets (#297, #298,
  #302); leftover debt goes on #285.

  Since yesterday evening: 13 PRs merged, 6 deployed, 3 new tickets created.
  Review caught real problems before they went live:
  - three ways bots could drain the shared mail quota;
  - German translations about to be lost, three separate times;
  - a login token that worked on more of the site than intended;
  - a live price shown to approved partners on a quote-only product.

  What went wrong:
  1. My two deploys caused short outages (about 30 seconds and about 3 minutes)
     because files were copied over the live site one by one. Fixed by #298;
     tonight's deploy is its first real run.
  2. #246 sat idle for about 4½ hours, because a Codex job failed at start and I
     missed it. Now covered by the watcher change and Codex moving to you.
  3. The same mistakes keep coming back each round. Translations typed straight
     into generated files, branches rebased onto newer work without rebuilding
     translations, bot protection rewritten from scratch. Reviews catch them,
     but each costs an extra round and extra tokens.

  What I'd change next: add those lessons to the shared rules every executor
  reads first, so they're done right the first time:
  - translations go through the source file and the build commands;
  - after a rebase, rebuild translations and prove nothing was lost;
  - reuse the existing bot protection and atomic limiter;
  - capture screenshots for pixel comparison the reliable way.

  That should cut most reviews to one round.

  What do you want to dig into: the review depth, the decisions I've been making
  alone, the deploy safety, or something else?

✻ Brewed for 22s · done 9:46 AM

❯ No offense, but I like to ask, do we really need to back up the whole thing   
  before we actually deploy? You know for a backup it will cost me like 30      
  minutes or so, because it's up to 4.6G as I remember. That's pretty long      
  time. I wonder if we could actually cut the process.                          

⏺ It's a fair question, and the answer is no. Most of that backup doesn't
  protect against what a deploy can actually break.

  What a deploy can damage:
  - Plugin code, the two must-use plugins and the theme's functions.php: since
    #298, the deploy itself keeps the previous version on the server, and just 
    cutover rollback-deploy swaps it back in seconds. A full backup adds nothing
    here.
  - Database: deploys only touch it lightly, through plugin activation,
    settings, and language packs. That's worth a snapshot. The database is about
    457 MB, mostly Genie's vector table, and the dump can be made on the server
    in a minute or two with nothing downloaded.
  - Uploads (about 4 GB): a code deploy never touches these. Copying them to
    your Mac on every deploy is most of the 30–60 minutes, and it protects
    nothing a deploy can break.

  What I propose:
  1. Before every deploy: a quick database snapshot kept on the server. The
     rollback copy of the code is already automatic. Total about 1–2 minutes
     instead of 30–60.
  2. Full backup to your Mac only when it matters: before bulk data changes
     (imports, mass edits, migrations), and on a regular schedule such as
     weekly. Make it incremental, so each run copies only what changed since the
     last one: minutes instead of the full 4.6 GB.
  3. Update the rule in AGENTS.md ("先备份、后读回", back up first, read back
     after) to say exactly this: code deploy = database snapshot + automatic
     code rollback; data changes = full backup.

  The tool change is a small Codex ticket: a quick backup mode, incremental full
  backups, and a guard that requires a fresh database snapshot before any
  deploy. I'd make it your first Codex ticket after #246.

  Tonight's backup: it started at 01:26 and is about two-thirds done. I'd let it
  finish this one time, since tonight is the first run of the new one-step
  deploy and an extra safety net costs only about 15 more minutes. If you'd
  rather not wait, I can stop it and take a database snapshot on the server
  instead.

  Shall I go ahead: create the ticket, write its prompt for you, and update the
  AGENTS.md rule?

› Message from general-purpose
  **Spec review: PR #306 (#305)**

  The PR meets the decision as the owner amended it. Two things I'd count as
  missing, nothing is scope creep, and one wording claim is false as written.

  **(a) Missing or partial**
  1. **Weekly full backup has no reminder.** The spec says "完整备份另每周一次"
  and the decision says "When a full backup is required: … weekly". Nothing
  checks or prompts for it. No launchd or cron job exists, and `deploy` does not
  warn when the newest complete `cutover-*` backup is older than 7 days.
  Smallest fix: `deploy` prints a note (or stops) when `latest_complete()` is
  more than 7 days old.
  2. **`schema --write` writes to the database with no backup.** The AGENTS rule
  says "数据写入…前做完整备份". `schema --write` drafts SEO rows, which is a
  data write, but it calls neither `_backup` nor a snapshot. `data`, `hygiene`
  and `plugins` all take their own full backup. The impact is small (drafts are
  reversible), but the rule says it should.
  3. **`restore-snapshot` does not snapshot the current database before
  overwriting it.** The just recipe also forces `--write`, so it has no dry run.
  Taking `snapshot.take()` first is cheap: 14 s and about 6 MB. Recommended.
  4. **Other write paths are fine.**
     - `probe` runs `deploy`; `deploy` and the drill (`snapshot_home=self.home`)
  both snapshot before the first upload.
     - `resume-deploy` and `rollback-deploy` only rename code back, and the
  inode manifest guards them.
     - `services --run` publishes to the VPS, whose `release.sh` keeps earlier
  versions behind the `current` symlink. It writes no site options; it only
  reports them.

  **(b) Scope creep:** none. The snapshot moved to the Mac and keeps 20 instead
  of 5 because of the amendment. Issue #305's Decision text still says
  `~/backups/linker-db/` and "keep 5"; I recommend editing the issue so it
  matches.

  **(c) Implemented but wrong**
  1. **"备份只存本机，不占服务器空间" (AGENTS §5)** is literally false. The same
  line relies on "原子切换保留上一版代码", and per spec 17 that previous code
  sits on the host in `~/deploy-prev/<id>/` (one batch). I'd keep it and add a
  carve-out: "上一版代码（约 28 MB，仅一批）留在主机 `~/deploy-prev/`
  供即时回滚，不算备份". The owner's goal is space, and one ~28 MB batch is
  small.
  2. **`wp_genie_chunks`: excluding it is safe.** `git grep genie_chunks
  origin/main -- plugins mu-plugins services/genie/src` finds nothing. The only
  hits are tests and `tools/pull`'s loader, which reads full backups. WordPress
  tables have no foreign keys, so leaving the table untouched on restore causes
  no consistency problem.
  3. **Spec caveat to add.** The spec says "导入只替换快照里有的表". Restore
  drops and recreates only the tables in the dump, so tables created after the
  snapshot (for example by a new plugin's activation) survive with their rows.
  Spec 17 should also say to run `rollback-deploy` alongside a restore so the
  code and the database match again.

  Files: `/Users/chesterjohn/Documents/de-linker/tools/cutover/src/linker_cutove
  r/snapshot.py`, `/Users/chesterjohn/Documents/de-linker/tools/cutover/src/link
  er_cutover/cli.py`,
  `/Users/chesterjohn/Documents/de-linker/tools/cutover/cutover.just`,
  `/Users/chesterjohn/Documents/de-linker/AGENTS.md`,
  `/Users/chesterjohn/Documents/de-linker/docs/specs/17-cutover.md`.

⏺ The Spec axis is in, and every point holds:
  - Weekly reminder: nothing reminds about the weekly full backup.
  - schema --write: it writes data without any backup.
  - restore-snapshot: it overwrites the database without first saving the
    current one.
  - AGENTS.md wording: "only on the Mac" isn't literally true, because the 28 MB
    previous-code copy stays on the server.
  - Restore caveat: a restore leaves alone any table created after the snapshot.

› Message from general-purpose
  **Standards review of PR #306.** Checked against `pr306` (an archive copy).
  ruff check, ruff format, and mypy strict all pass. pytest: 134 passed, 1
  failed. The failure was
  `test_probe_samples_slowly_until_switch_then_densely_through_readback`, which
  passed on two reruns, so it is a timing flake. This PR doesn't touch that
  test, but AGENTS §5 says what we find this round we fix this round
  (本轮发现本轮修).

  **Security and correctness checks (all hold)**
  - The password is passed only as `MYSQL_PWD` inside the script running on the
  host. The real-shell test asserts `"secret" not in arguments`.
  - `--ignore-table="$name.$prefix"'genie_chunks'` expands to
  `shop.wpx_genie_chunks`, verified by the real bash run.
  - `finally: partial.unlink(missing_ok=True)` cleans up the partial file on
  every failure path.
  - Pruning only touches names that match `NAME`.
  - `_checked` blocks `../` in restore names.
  - `--link-dest` is passed as an absolute path and only when that directory
  exists. The chunk mapping is right: a file chunk links against
  `(link_dest/path).parent`, a directory chunk against `link_dest/path`.
  - rsync is called with `-az --delete` and no `--inplace`, so it writes to temp
  files and renames them. Older backups' inodes can't be changed.
  - `latest_complete` only picks a backup that has its manifest.

  **Hard violations**
  1. `tools/cutover/tests/test_backup.py`: the hard-link test runs only the
  local `rsync` branch. The remote chunk branch's `link_dest` mapping in
  `target.py` `copy_out` has no test. This breaks AGENTS §5, which requires
  real-fixture tests at the layer that changed (改动所在层的真实夹具测试).
  2. `snapshot.py`: there are two `# noqa: SLF001` comments, but
  `pyproject.toml` only selects `["E4","E7","E9","F","I","B","UP"]`, so they
  suppress nothing. Behind them, `snapshot` calls `backup._stream`, which is
  private. The fix is to make `_stream` public, not to add a suppression.

  **Judgement calls**
  - `restore-snapshot` replaces the whole production database but takes no
  backup first. The new AGENTS line counts an import (导入) as a data write that
  needs a backup beforehand. Also, the dry run (no `--write`) doesn't validate
  the name or check the file exists.
  - `NAME.match` with `^…$` also accepts a trailing `\n`. `NAME.fullmatch` is
  the idiomatic fix.
  - In `take`, if a newer-named file already exists (for example after clock
  skew), pruning can delete the snapshot just taken. `final.stat()` then raises
  `FileNotFoundError`.

  **Smells (Fowler)**
  - **Duplicated Code:** `snapshot.take` repeats `backup.create`'s "stream dump
  → `readable_gzip` → sha256" sequence, and `snapshot.restore` repeats
  `backup.restore`'s "`_stream(restore_script)` + `cache flush`".
  - **Divergent Change / misplaced ownership:** `cli.DATA_HOME =
  snapshot_module.DEFAULT_HOME` makes the snapshot module own the shared data
  root. The comment on it still points to docs/local-stack.md.
  - **Data Clumps:** `deploy(..., snapshot_home=..., take_snapshot=...)` takes
  two parameters for one concern. `take_snapshot` exists only as a test seam,
  while the autouse fixture already monkeypatches `snapshot.take`, so this leans
  toward Speculative Generality.
  - **Primitive Obsession (minor):** `Snapshot.bytes` shadows the builtin type
  name.

  Number sourcing is fine: `KEEP = 20`, 817.9 MB and 6,200,721 bytes all have
  measured-source comments.

✻ Cogitated for 10s · done 11:33 AM

❯ Why don't you run it yourself?                                                

⏺ I can run the SSH command, but this step needs the mailbox password, and I'm
  not allowed to handle passwords. That's a firm safety rule for me: I don't
  type credentials into anything, write them into files, or repeat them, even
  when you give them to me and say it's fine. It exists so that a mistake on my
  side, or text planted in a page I read, can never leak your logins.

  Typing it here or with ! in this chat doesn't get around it either: the
  password would land in this conversation's transcript.

  So the safe way is the one I gave you: your own Terminal window. The password
  goes straight from your keyboard into a file only your hosting account can
  read. The site reads it from there, and I only ever check that the file
  exists, never what's in it. It takes under a minute, and you only do it once.

› Message from general-purpose
  **Standards review, PR #309 (read-only)**

  **Hard violations**
  1. **Third-party photos in a public repo.**
  `services/genie/eval/attachments/README.md` gives a source URL and sha256 for
  each photo (installer blog, sunriver-inverter.com, a technosolars listing,
  powerforum, two Reddit posts, diysolarforum). It records no licence and no
  permission for any of them. `gh repo view` reports the repo as **PUBLIC**, so
  about 7 copyrighted images are being republished. Only the GoodWe label, the
  EcoFlow upload and the derived thumbnail are our own material.
  2. **pypdfium2 is used from more than one thread.** `attachments._pdf` runs
  inside the sync `def assistant_turn`, which FastAPI runs in a threadpool, so
  two turns with PDFs can parse at the same time. PDFium is not thread-safe,
  even across different documents, and nothing in `src/` takes a lock around it.
  Concurrent turns can crash the process.
  3. **Numbers without a source comment (§4).** These have no source: `Rating`,
  `brand`, `model` and `failed` `max_length=200` (schema.py), `_short`'s
  `[:200]`, `ratings[:60]` and `max_length=60`, `_message`'s `[:300]` and `id`
  pattern `\d{1,3}`. The 6/16/20 MiB, 3 files, 20 per hour, 1.69 MP, 4 rendered
  pages, 60 pages, 30 s and 6 workers are all sourced.

  **Security (judgement calls)**
  - **`Attachments::check` (PHP):** it runs `base64_decode` on every file before
  any length check, and only after the full JSON body (about 27 MB of base64 at
  the 20 MiB cap) is already in memory. Files refused for size or type never
  use any of the hourly allowance. A guest can therefore repeat oversized
  requests without being throttled, and each refusal writes a `wc_get_logger`
  line. Fixes:
    - check `strlen($data)` against `ceil(max/3)*4` before decoding;
    - count refusals against the allowance.

    Also, the `take` loop uses up allowance for earlier files when a later file
  gets the 429.
  - **Service:** the `Upload.data` cap applies to each file, so a direct POST
  body can reach about 67 MB before `MAX_TURN_BYTES` is checked. The turn
  endpoint has no service authentication, so the limit depends only on how the
  VPS is exposed. PDF parsing has no wall-clock limit.
  - **Turn log:** `turns.sqlite` keeps each reading's text (up to 60k
  characters) and records all of them again on every turn. Spec 13 documents
  this, but such text is not deleted with the conversation and can contain PII
  from PDF text layers.
  - **Covered and correct:**
    - magic bytes are checked in PHP, Python and TS;
    - the WebP check also requires `WEBP` at offset 8;
    - Pillow refuses images over 50 MP before decoding them;
    - EXIF and ICC data are removed on the server as well as in the browser;
    - render size is limited by area;
    - no PDF forms or JS run;
    - readings come only from conversation meta;
    - the prompt (EN/DE and `_user_prompt`) treats file text as data, never
  instructions, and values must be cited as `attN.nK`;
    - logs carry only reason codes and ids, never keys.

  **Model routing (§5):** images go only to deepseek-flash through the chat
  model's key and base URL, with no fallback and explicit 4xx/5xx errors.
  Nothing is added to OpenRouter. If `GENIE_BASE_URL` is overridden, images
  follow it, and nothing checks that the host is api.deepseek.com.

  **Code smells (judgement calls)**
  - **Duplicated Code / Feature Envy:** the key and base URL are resolved from
  env in three places (`agent.build_model`, `app.vision()`,
  `eval/nameplates.py`). `app.py` and the eval script both import the private
  `_env_with_credentials`.
  - **Duplicated Code:** the magic-byte table exists in PHP, Python and TS.
  Tests lock the size limits together but not the magic-byte tables.
  - **Duplicated work:** `Thread::split` runs twice on the same stream, once in
  `record` and once in `Proxy::turn`.

› Message from general-purpose
  **Standards review, PR #310 (pr310 vs origin/main, docs/acceptance not
  reviewed). 2 hard violations, 4 judgement calls.**

  **Hard violations**

  1. **i18n catalogs were edited by hand; `just i18n-commerce` was not run.** In
  `languages/linker-commerce.pot` and `-de_DE_formal.po`, the new msgid
  "Account under review. Prices after approval." is appended at the end of each
  file. The recipe's `msgcat --sort-by-file` would have placed it with the other
  PriceDisplay entries. The old `msgid "Prices after approval"` is still an
  active entry in both files (.pot:936, .po:987), still pointing at
  `PriceDisplay.php:223 / ProductView.php:304`. No code uses that string any
  more, so `msgmerge` would have marked it obsolete. The compiled .mo is safe:
  comparing main and pr310 with stdlib gettext gives 828 → 829 entries, 0 lost,
  0 changed, 1 added.
  2. **Stale e2e test, which breaks 本轮发现本轮修.**
  `tests/e2e/specs/genie-prices.spec.ts:38` still has `pending: { en: 'Prices
  after approval', de: 'Preise nach Freigabe' }`, and lines 144–145 compare with
  `toBe`, an exact match. The PHP twin (`GeniePricesTest.php`) was updated;
  this spec was not, so it will fail.

  **Price authority**

  - **`AccountTemplates.php:784` (judgement: Middle Man, Divergent Change).**
  `apply_filters( 'linker_commerce_visitor_sees_prices', false ) ?
  get_formatted_order_total() : ''`. Spec 11 says the filter is how *other
  plugins* ask the rule; inside linker-commerce the code should use
  CommerceState directly. The bigger problem is that the row ignores
  `PriceDisplay::order_totals`/`has_unpriced_lines`. An approved customer's old
  inquiry with unpriced lines will still show "€0.00" in the row and in
  summary.html, while the detail totals hide it. That is a second, inconsistent
  total rule. For pending accounts, `summary.html` prints an "Amount" label with
  an empty value.
  - **`PriceDisplay::reason_text` (judgement).** When the reason is APPROVAL and
  the status is APPROVED, it returns the UNPRICED text, so a reason is
  reclassified outside `CommerceState`. It is small and tested, but it is a
  state rule living in the wording code.
  - **Duplicated Code / Shotgun Surgery (judgement).** The pending wording is
  mapped separately in `PriceDisplay::reason_text` and
  `ProductView::price_html`. Changing it touched 2 classes and 4 test files and
  still missed the e2e spec.

  **Dead code and naming**

  - Clean: no remaining callers of `pending.php`, `PendingGate::output`,
  `holds()`, or LoginGate's `card/badge/action`. Every hook priority has a
  source comment ("native priority 10"). The design pieces are in the right
  place: the card is an LC fragment and the styling is in WindPress `auth.css`.
  - Mysterious Name (judgement): `PendingGate` no longer blocks anything; it
  only shows a status notice. LoginGate's file docblock still says "The pending
  account page".
  - `PendingGateTest::other_states` has a row `'staff' => (6, APPROVED)` that
  does not actually set up a staff user.

  **Fixture (judgement)**

  `tests/e2e/fixtures/pending-account.php` is outside every phpcs ruleset and
  uses one-line `if {}` statements. Its setup and cleanup (throttle, test mode,
  limit rows) largely copy `public-inquiry.php`, which is duplicated code.
  Repeatability is guarded: setup refuses to run while an unrestored fixture
  exists.

  No legacy vocabulary was added.

⏺ Agent "Standards review of PR 310" finished · 2m 13s

› Message from general-purpose
  **Spec review of PR #310 (#222)**

  **Stability item (run10): not a code bug. It is a local object-cache artefact,
  and it cannot happen on live as live is set up today.**
  - The account card (`PendingGate::render`) and the product price
  (`CommerceState::current_approval_status` / `visitor_sees_prices`) read the
  same source: `Approval::status()` → `get_user_meta(uid, STATUS_META)`. Nothing
  else stores approval state: no static memo, transient, Woo session or
  customer-object copy. `Approval::set()` calls `update_user_meta`, which
  already deletes the `user_meta` cache entry. No extra invalidation is needed
  while the cache is coherent.
  - How it happened: the local stack runs Redis Object Cache in graceful mode
  (`WP_REDIS_GRACEFUL=true`) on Redis database 0, shared by all instances
  (`docs/local-stack.md:40`). The timeline in
  `diagnostics/cache-availability.json`:
    - approval at 03:12:12.18;
    - a Predis read error on a web request at 03:12:13;
    - product screenshot at 03:12:14.45.
    The product request fell back to the in-request cache, read the database,
  and saw "approved". The next account request reconnected and read a stale
  "pending" entry still in Redis. That entry survived either because the CLI's
  cache delete was lost while Redis was failing, or because a concurrent request
  wrote it back. CLI errors are not in the web log, so which one is unproven.
  - Live, read-only checks:
    - `wp cache type` = Default, and there is no `object-cache.php` (redis-cache
  is active, but its drop-in is absent).
    - The LiteSpeed Cache plugin is inactive and there is no
  `advanced-cache.php`. Only a leftover `LSCACHE` block remains in `.htaccess`.
    - `/my-account/` returns `cache-control: no-cache, must-revalidate,
  max-age=0, private`.
  - What to do: no code fix in this PR. Close the item using this evidence. The
  risk only comes back if production Redis is enabled later (the #17 cutover).

  **(a) Missing / partial**
  - "线上 EN/DE × 1440/390：注册 → 保持登录 → … → 批准后同一会话里价格出现": the
  live walk has not been done, and there is no live/local comparison of an
  approved account.
  - AGENTS 2026-10-17: "'限制'用于告诉客户信息不够…原因给客户看", and #227:
  "限制原因是给客户看的待补清单". A restricted customer's account page shows no
  status and no reason. The reason appears only above `/proforma-request/` (and
  in the email). That matches #246. But this PR makes the account page the home
  of the status notice and gives it to pending accounts only, so a restricted
  customer can't see what to fix there. Rejected matches #246/#227: sign-in is
  blocked and `destroy_all()` ends the session.

  **(b) Scope creep**
  - None in the code. Hiding the inquiry-row total behind the shared price
  filter and showing "Price on request" on old inquiries after approval both
  follow directly from the change. Evidence is bulky: run8–14 and reboot15/16
  JSON make up most of the 222 files.

  **(c) Implemented but wrong**
  - Pixel attribution. Rule §5: "未声明的差异像素即 FAIL". The PR touches no
  guest surface (product, login, register or category), so these 8 failures are
  not caused by it. But `pixel/README.md` hands the 13,061 px DE header
  difference to "现有页头一致性票 #229", and #229 is CLOSED (merged in #294 at
  01:50Z). #246 and #252 are not valid open owners either. More likely, local
  main is ahead of what is deployed on live. Re-shoot after deploy, or compare
  local main against this branch, before calling the gate pre-existing.

⏺ 这段时间我这边主要在做下面这些，有几项还在后台跑。

  1. #308 部署时的报错窗口：已做完
  - 原因（已查实）： 线上 PHP 每 2
    秒才检查一次代码文件有没有更新。所以切换后最多 2
    秒内，个别请求还会用旧代码，今早那 2 条报错就是这么来的。
  - 解决办法： 部署在切换后的同一瞬间，用一次性令牌通知网站清空 PHP
    缓存。令牌只在服务器上生成，用完即删，其他账户看不到。
  - 测试： 用真实的 PHP 服务器做了端到端测试，全部通过（147 项）。PR 是 #311。

  2. 合并和部署：后台进行中

  CI 通过后会自动合并 #311，接着部署。这次部署会带上已合并但还没上线的 #243（让
  Linker 联系我），同时检验 #308 修好的效果：部署日志里应该看到"缓存已清"，报错
  日志里不应再新增条目。完成后我告诉你结果。
